# V1 Launch-Readiness Audit — Basic Modeling UX (LIVING DOCUMENT)

> **Status**: LIVING · **Owner**: engine · **Started**: 2026-07-02 · **Target**: v1 launch (next week)
> **Companion plan**: `docs/04-reference/V1-LAUNCH-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md` (phased/subphased, side-by-side).
> **Feeds from**: `ADR-0098` (element-lifecycle conformance audit), `ADR-0099` (host-wall freeze fix),
> `ELEMENT-LIFECYCLE-REMEDIATION-PLAN.md` (element detail + Q-queue).
> **Governance**: this is a launch-readiness rollup, NOT a `*-AUDIT.md` contract-derivative — it references the
> canonical C-contracts (`docs/02-decisions/contracts/`) and never redefines them.

## How to use this document
Every issue the founder reports (past + future) is logged in **§2 Issue Log** with an `L-NN` id and mapped to a
functional area in **§3**. Append new issues to the bottom of §2 and tick the area table in §3. The companion
plan turns these into phased work. **Nothing is dropped.**

Verdict legend: **OK** (verified working) · **GAP** (works but diverges/incomplete) · **BROKEN** (reproduced
defect) · **N/V** (needs source verification — file cited).

---

## §1 Scope — the "basic modeling must be perfect" surface

The v1 promise is that an architect can, flawlessly and responsively: **navigate the camera**, **create elements**,
**move/rotate/edit elements** (dimensions, materials, hosting), **select**, **create & manage views**
(plan / 3D / elevation / section / sheets), **annotate & dimension**, **undo/redo**, and **save/load** — on both
an empty project and a heavy one, without freezes or stutter.

---

Exists for walls (WALL TYPE) but **doesn't work for existing placed elements** — select an element → replace with a different type | Editing/Types | SHIPPED | ADR-0105 §FEAT-ELEMENT-CHANGE-TYPE (batch 3) |
| L-18 | founder | **Duplicate furniture on open** — moving a sofa reveals an identical one underneath (collab catch-up re-creates already-placed furniture; dedup covers rooms/slabs/floors but not furniture) | Save/Load/Collab | **FIXED (f8b64a39, live)** | §FIX-CATCHUP-DUPLICATE-CREATE — **ROOT CAUSE (2026-07-10):** TWO gaps. (1) The reproducing one: `CreateFurnitureCommand.execute()` registered the element in `bimManager` but NEVER in `elementRegistry`, while every other Create* family does (`CreateWallCommand.ts:269` -> `'wall'`). `RemoteCommandDispatcher.isAlreadyAppliedCreate` keys dedup on `elementRegistry.getStoreType(id) !== undefined`, so the replayed `CREATE_FURNITURE` was never skipped -> re-executed on catch-up (re-minting a fresh UUID, since `payload.id` was absent) -> the duplicate sofa underneath. That is exactly why walls dedup and furniture does not. (2) Structural: the dedup gate `type.startsWith('CREATE_')/'BATCH_CREATE_'` + `targetIds` is blind to the ENTIRE dotted-bus create family (`furniture.create`, `lighting.create`, `wall.batch.create`, …) whose ids live in `payload.id` / per-entry ids. FIX: register furniture (+ dining-chair children) in `elementRegistry` on execute, unregister on undo; add a dotted-bus branch (`type.endsWith('.create')`) with a `collectBusCreateTargetIds` helper (conservative: skip only when EVERY extracted id is registered; non-minting verbs like `wall.createOpening` correctly excluded); bridge bus-placed furniture via `initTools §FT-FURNITURE`. Legacy branch preserved byte-for-byte. Gate: 18/18 idempotency (+9 new), 29/29 furniture suites, root tsc exit 0. RESIDUAL: other bus families (lighting/handrail/beam/curtainwall) still need their own `elementRegistry` registration for their dedup to fire; and bus commands are never socket-broadcast to `project_command_log` (only the legacy `commandManager` path is), so live cross-user sync of bus-placed elements does not occur — see L-206. **SUPERSEDED RECONCILE NOTE (2026-07-05):** The `§DUPLICATE-ROOMS-PERSIST` dedup (`RemoteCommandDispatcher.isAlreadyAppliedCreate`, `apps/editor/src/engine/RemoteCommandDispatcher.ts:72-79`) is type-agnostic but keys on `type.startsWith('CREATE_')`/`BATCH_CREATE_` + populated `targetIds`, applied only to serialized commands replayed through `RemoteCommandDispatcher`/`commandManager.onCommandExecuted`. Production furniture placement dispatches the **bus** `furniture.create` (`CreateFurnitureHandler`, lowercase dotted type) from `FurnitureDragDropHandler`/`FurniturePlanToolHandler`, bridged to the legacy store via `furniture.created` events (`initTools §FT-FURNITURE`) — it does NOT flow through the legacy `commandManager` broadcast as a `CREATE_FURNITURE` serialized command, so the generic dedup demonstrably does not cover it. No furniture idempotency test exists (`RemoteCommandDispatcherIdempotency.test.ts` covers room/stair/slab only). Also founder-HELD (line ~298). |

| L-27 | founder | **Wall T-junction STILL shows an "arrow" spike** — interior partition meeting a wall in a T; L-12's `§FIX-WALL-TJUNCTION-BUTT` (JunctionResolverV2) shipped but is INSUFFICIENT for the real thin-partition case (black wedge in 3D + arrow tip in plan). Re-opened. | Geometry/Walls | BROKEN→FIXED | ADR-0055 `§FIX-WALL-TJUNCTION-BUTT-2` (batch 3). **Real root cause = the ring-sweep PIVOT, not the classification.** L-12 correctly reclassified host→passthrough (guest side-corners butt on the host near face) — but `applyRingSweep` still wrote a centreline PIVOT vertex at the host centreline for the T-attacher and `WallFootprint2D` inserted it BETWEEN the two near-face corners → a triangular tongue from the near face (z=+halfT) DOWN to the host centreline (z=0): the founder's 3D wedge / plan chevron. L-12's `§WALL-BODY-INNER-FACE` suppressed that pivot ONLY when the host was ≥1.5× thicker, so an equal-thickness partition-into-wall T still spiked. Fix: suppress the centreline pivot for EVERY real endpoint abutting a passthrough (a T is not an X), thickness-independent; AND re-point the ring-sweep pivot for every clean 1-real-end T onto the host-centreline foot so a guest ending on the host inner face butts flush (no half-thickness gap). Plan chevron = projection of the fixed 3D footprint (`EdgeProjectorService` has no T-logic) → auto-resolves. Layered/opening walls use the legacy `WallJoinResolver._clampEndToShellInnerFace` which already butt-clamps body-Ts (arrow is V2-footprint-specific). **Cluster sub-case `§FIX-WALL-CLUSTER-DEGENERATE`:** the founder's other repro is an L-corner (two walls co-terminate) that ALSO receives a THIRD wall at the SAME vertex → black triangular spike. Reproduced in a footprint harness: a genuine 3-wall node of FULL-LENGTH walls tiles cleanly (all positive-area, shared pivot); the spike is a DEGENERATE cluster member — a wall shorter than the 0.20 m band whose BOTH endpoints snap to one node, so the ring sweep hinges both ends on one pivot → a bow-tie / negative-area (inverted-normal, area −0.03) footprint that also distorts the L-corner. This stub (0.158 m) clears the legacy 0.15 m length gate and the legacy 0.12 m band never doubles it, so only the V2 path renders it. Fix: detect a wall doubled in a cluster, STRIP it from the sweep (the L-corner walls then render clean) and mark its `WallMiter`/`WallFootprint` `invalid` → `WallFragmentBuilder.buildWall` skips its mesh at the shared `§WJR-INVALID` checkpoint. Genuine N-way full-length nodes unaffected (no false positive). Footprint-level regression tests added (thin-partition T + L-corner-plus-stub cluster: 17 cases green; 114 geometry-wall + 67 V2 tests green). C15/C11. |

Change type below (optional)." Type dropdown stays apply-on-change for live switching; ESC-cancel, wall-mode, align guide untouched; 3D path unchanged. | L-30 | founder | **(follow-up to L-29) Door/Window-MOVE preview dimensions (along wall vector)** — for wall-hosted elements show move dimensions **along** the host wall's vector (hosted elements slide only along the wall). | Editing/Move/Hosting | FIXED | `§FEAT-HOSTED-MOVE-DIMENSIONS` — new pure `computeHostedMoveDimensions` (`packages/core-app-model/src/geometry/hostedMoveDimensions.ts`, 9 tests): works in the host wall's own 1D parameter space (offset in metres from `baseLine[0]`), returns the along-wall gap on each side (`start`/`end`) from the moving opening's near EDGE to the nearest reference — the wall end OR the facing edge of the nearest adjacent opening on the SAME wall, whichever is closer. Transient overlay (read-only, no store writes P6, no new rAF P3 — reuses the existing overlay schedule), cleared on drag end/cancel. Descriptor shape mirrors L-29's `WallMoveDimension`. | L-31 | founder | **Cross-level REFERENCES (Revit-like, better)** — when drawing walls on an UPPER floor, PRYZM should intelligently offer sound references to the **slab corners** (which are defined by the lower floor's walls) to place the 1st + subsequent points. Also fixes the `SnapBoundsError: SpatialGrid.getCellKeysForBounds total cell count 11039838 exceeds cap` seen when snapping far from origin on L1. | Modeling/Snapping/Refs | FIXED | `§FEAT-SLAB-CORNER-REFS` + `§FIX-SNAP-BOUNDS-OVERFLOW` (ADR-0112). (1) `SlabSnapProvider` extended: emits slab-polygon vertices as ENDPOINT (the corners), edge midpoints as MIDPOINT, edge nearest-points as EDGE; level-gated to the active draw floor via a lazy `getActiveLevelId()` accessor. Wired by default in `SnapManager.createWithDefaults()` — falls back to read-only `window.slabStore` + `window.projectContext.activeLevelId` so WallTool (which forwards only `{gridStore}`) gets the reference untouched; reuses the existing SnapVisualizer indicator + rankCandidates pipeline (composes with L-26 guide/ortho). (2) root cause of the 11039838-cell throw: `SpatialGrid.getCellKeysForBounds` SOLID-FILLED an oversized/degenerate (origin-spanning or geolocated far-from-origin) element AABB, and `insert()` — unlike `query()` — did NOT catch the `SnapBoundsError`, so it propagated out of the wall-draw flow. Fix = degrade oversized fills to the AABB's ≤8 CORNER cells (bounded, snapping stays correct at corners/endpoints, never throws on insert); non-finite bounds still throw and `query()` degrades to `[]`. 3 tests (far-from-origin under cap; slab corners as candidates; draw-point snaps to corner) + level-gate/fallback/EDGE tests. C06/snapping |

No persistence-contract change → no ADR. Tests: editor `underlayDeleteCommand` (3 — registration + delete teardown + undo restore) + `underlayPersistenceStorage` (4 — 300 KB raster→IDB with lean localStorage under a 4 KB budget, legacy migration, clear, metadata-only read). |

| L-58 | founder | **"Overlay a plan / PDF" onboarding flow doesn't visibly work** — the Site-plan overlay PANEL + controls render (Move/Rotate/Scale/2-pt calibrate/Opacity/Lock/Hide/Remove/"Use this placement (set Project North)") but the founder can't see the uploaded image/PDF overlaid on the satellite map, nor complete the flow (Step 2 → trace plot). | L-59 | founder | **SSGI causes constant flickering in WebGPU + TRAA black-flash on selection** — (1) with WebGPU active, ALL elements FLICKER constantly; historically this was SSGI being on (SSGINode). Make **SSGI OFF by default** (keep the toggle). (2) TRAA: clicking an element used to make rendered elements vanish; now they go BLACK for ~1s then return with no change — the TRAA colour-filter rebuild on selection flashes black. | Camera/Render/Post-FX | FIXED | `§FIX-SSGI-DEFAULT-OFF-TRAA-SELECT-FLASH` (C04 / ADR-0111). **Root cause:** the `cinematic` + `balanced` tiers in `SceneQualityTierManager` set `ssgi:true, traa:true`, and `initScene` unconditionally called `renderPipelineManager.activateSSGI()` at startup — so on WebGPU, SSGINode's per-frame denoise temporal accumulation flickered every element (issue 1). The tier's TRAA hook (`RenderingPipelineCoordinator._onTierTraa → rpm.activateTRAA/deactivateTRAA`) rebuilds the pipeline (`_rebuildPipelineWithCurrentState` → new WebGPU `RenderPipeline` → shader PSO recompile ≈ ~1s BLACK frame) on ANY tier transition; scene/selection-driven `applyTierForMeshCount` re-evaluations therefore flashed the viewport black on select (issue 2). **Fix:** SSGI + TRAA now default OFF at every tier (cinematic/balanced set `ssgi:false, traa:false`, matching performance/survival), the startup `activateSSGI()` call is removed (outlines still activate → selection highlight intact), and the RenderRail SSGI toggle defaults OFF — both remain fully user-opt-in via `rpm.activateSSGI/activateTRAA`. Because the tier no longer drives SSGI/TRAA on, no tier/selection-driven rebuild-to-black; `setSelectedObjects()` only mutates the live outline arrays (never rebuilds). (b) New `EdgeProjectorService.projectElementsInto(targetDrawing, viewDef, dirtyGroups, dirtyIds)` projects ONLY the dirty groups (via the SAME `project()` path → pixel-identical, no divergent code) into a throwaway drawing, then **idempotently grafts** only the lines tagged with a dirty element's UUID onto the already-warm cached drawing (clone-in; remove-then-add so the 30 ms + 300 ms drivers cannot duplicate linework). (c) Both drivers take the graft fast-path when eligible + a warm drawing exists + native-only plan view, and **self-heal to a full reprojection on any error** (fallback = prior behaviour, zero-regression by construction). A single wall/slab add is now O(dirty) — only the new element re-projects; every other element re-uses its cached projection (no whole-drawing dispose, no full symbol/HLR re-run, no double full-projection). Doors/windows/furniture creates (symbol-bearing) intentionally keep the full path for correctness (documented follow-up: per-element symbol inject to extend the graft). | L-76 | founder | **New wall onto an existing L-joint: PREVIEW ≠ RESULT (skewed/tapered)** — draw a NEW wall (perpendicular, 100 mm, snapped near the corner) onto/near an existing L-shape joint; the live PREVIEW is clean (perpendicular, snapped to the corner) but the EXECUTED wall is mangled — tapered/skewed, not joining the L cleanly. This is the concrete geometry that unblocks **L-63 Part-1** (the resolver-trim discriminator). | Geometry/Walls | BROKEN→FIXED | `§FIX-NEWWALL-LCORNER-SKEW` (ADR-0055). **Root cause (verified by resolveLevel repro):** the new wall C, drawn PERPENDICULAR to arm A, is therefore PARALLEL to arm B. Added a lateral-coincidence gate: the perpendicular offset between the two candidate walls' junction endpoints must be within a half-thickness band (`max(0.05, max(t_i,t_j)/2)`). C (0.313 m offset) is now correctly NOT a pass-through → it routes to the T-into-corner path and `_applyT` butts it on A's face at its OWN foot (perpendicular, length preserved). The genuine collinear pass-through (offset ≈ 0, incl. resi `_repro_passthrough` — 290 mm partition onto a committed-corner consensus ON the through-line) is byte-unchanged. Discriminator = *lateral coincidence*, exactly the "consensus-on-host-body vs off-a-different-host" split, and it is derivable from the cluster (no downstream dependency). Detection/footprint frame — the stored baseline is only trimmed toward the host it truly abuts, never skewed off-axis. Gated `__pryzmPassThroughCoincidentGate` (default ON). Tests: geometry-wall `WallJoinResolver.newWallLCornerSkew` (5 — executed baseline perpendicular + on-axis + length preserved; positive-area non-self-intersecting footprint; V2 preview == executed; **stable on reopen** re-resolving persisted baselines; resi pass-through unaffected). 140 geometry-wall + 107 ai-host tests green. | L-81 | founder | **Project DUPLICATION doesn't work** — duplicating a project fails / the duplicate is not created correctly (the duplicate opens broken/empty). | Persistence/Project-lifecycle | BROKEN→FIXED | `§FIX-PROJECT-DUPLICATE-OPEN`. **Root cause:** duplication created a new project id + metadata but copied **no snapshot**. Server `projectStore.duplicateProject` did a bare `INSERT INTO projects …` with **zero** version rows, and the client `ProjectHub.duplicateProject` saved the new meta with `versionCount:0` **without** copying the source's local `versionRepository` history under the new id. So on open, the local-first path (`PlatformShell.setProjectContext` → `getVersions(newId)`) found nothing and fell through to `GET /api/projects/:id/latest-version`, which returned `{version:null}` / 404 (`[persistence.tier.streamLoad] Server 404`) → the duplicate opened empty/broken. **Fix (two authoritative halves):** (1) **Server** — `duplicateProject` now copies the project row AND its **latest** version snapshot atomically in one `withTransaction`, via a pure `INSERT INTO project_versions … SELECT … FROM project_versions WHERE project_id=$source ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 1` (JSONB duplicated inside Postgres, no Node round-trip) and sets `version_count=1`; the no-pool in-memory dev path is guarded so it no longer throws. Latest-only (not the full 20-deep history) keeps memory bounded — the open path restores only the latest, and history re-accrues on the duplicate's future saves. (2) **Client** — new `LocalVersionRepository.duplicateInto(sourceId, targetId, name)` deep-copies the source's local version history under the new id (re-keying each record + embedded snapshot `projectId`/`projectName`, marked `synced`), called from `ProjectHub.duplicateProject` BEFORE saving the meta (meta `versionCount` = copied count) so the local-first open restores the source's elements instantly / offline. Returns 0 when the source was never opened on this device — the server-side copy then carries the open. Tests: server `projectStore.test.js` (2 source-shape guards — transactional version copy + no-pool guard) and editor `projectDuplicateOpen.test.ts` (3 — duplicate has a loadable latest snapshot carrying the source's elements under the new id; source history unmutated; empty-source → 0/no-op). C13/C05; C05 §1.1 atomic transaction. |
| L-82 | founder | **Window dimension change beyond wall bounds destroys the opening permanently + window undeletable** — while changing a window's dimensions, if the window FRAME goes outside the host wall, the wall stops being cut and the OPENING disappears; then even when the dimensions are brought BACK within the wall, the opening does NOT reappear; and such windows CANNOT be deleted. | Creation/Hosted-elements/Openings | BROKEN→FIXED | `§FIX-WINDOW-OOB-OPENING-RESTORE` (C15 hosted elements). **Root cause — NO wall-extent guard on window dimension edits.** Only the MOVE path (`SetWindowOffsetCommand`) validated placement via `WallOccupancyStore.canPlace`; every DIMENSION path (`UpdateWindowParameterCommand` from the Doors/Windows panel, `UpdateElementParameterCommand`/`window.setSize` from the property inspector, `UpdateWindowWidthCommand`) checked only `> 0`. So a width / height / offset / sill edit could push the opening span `[offset, offset+width] × [sill, sill+height]` past the host wall. The failure triad then follows once the hosted element desyncs — it survives in the external `windowStore` (drives the selectable 3-D mesh) but is dropped from `WallStore.windows` / `wall.openings`: (1) the wall stops being cut (opening gone from `wall.openings`); (2) it never re-cuts because `WallStore.updateWindow` early-returns for a window missing from its internal map, so bringing the size back in bounds does nothing; (3) `DeleteElementCommand` resolves windows ONLY via `wallStore.getWindow` → an external-store-only window is unresolvable → `canExecute` rejects → undeletable. **Fix (three parts).** (1) GUARD/CLAMP — new pure `WallOccupancyStore.clampToWall(wall, dims)` keeps the frame span entirely inside the wall (width∈[MIN,wallLen], offset∈[0,wallLen−width]; height∈[MIN,wallH], sill∈[0,wallH−height]) — a width that fits is preserved by shifting the offset inward (mirrors WindowTool's placement clamp), only an over-wall width is itself shrunk. Applied at BOTH the command (`UpdateWindowParameterCommand` — clamps the patch before writing so the frame `windowStore` and opening `wallStore` get the SAME in-bounds values, incl. any derived offset shift, undoable) AND the universal store choke point (`WallStore.updateWindow` — every caller funnels through it). The out-of-bounds state is now impossible. (2) RE-CUT/RESTORE — `WallStore.updateWindow` self-heals: if the window's opening is missing from the wall it RE-CREATES it from the (clamped, in-bounds) window record, so the wall re-cuts instead of staying solid. (3) DELETE ROBUST — `DeleteElementCommand` gains an orphaned-hosted-element branch: `canExecute` + `execute` also resolve via the external `windowStore`/`doorStore`, so a window/door present only there is still deletable (frees the external record, drops any lingering wall opening, unregisters) and undo restores it. Do-NOT-touch honoured: no wall JOIN geometry edited (JunctionResolverV2/WallFootprint2D/WallJoinResolver/WallRebuildCoordinator untouched). A wall create fires BOTH, and the follow-up `REDETECT_ROOMS` (room type, not graft-safe) fires the coarse full path too. One driver's `invalidate(viewId)` disposes+DELETES the only cached drawing AND bumps the generation WHILE the other driver's `project()` is still in flight; that projection then completes tagged `staleGen<currentGen` → `ViewTechnicalDrawingCache.setIfCurrent()` REJECTED it → cache left EMPTY → blank plan. When the cache is non-empty the reject is preserved (never clobber newer-good with older). This makes the graft/redetect gen-race HARMLESS across ALL 4 `setIfCurrent` sites (PlanViewManager main + split, ViewDependencyTracker graft + full) WITHOUT disabling the L-65 graft. Regression test `packages/core-app-model/src/views/__tests__/planBlankStaleGen.test.ts` reproduces the staleGen<currentGen race and asserts the drawing is NEVER empty after a create (4 tests green). The miter NORMAL already matches V2 (both 45° at a 0.2×0.2 L), but when the door sits within a half-thickness (the 45° miter reach ≈ 100 mm) of the corner, the tiny wall sliver between the door jamb and the corner is shorter than the miter reach, so the miter's INNER cap vertex projects BACKWARD past the jamb into the door void → a self-intersecting triangular spike/notch in plan (reproduced: door 50 mm from corner → footprint minX 4.900 < jamb 4.950). **Fix:** in `buildMiterPrism`, clamp each projected cap vertex so it may extend PAST its own end (the legitimate outer overhang that meets the neighbour's face) but NEVER retreats past the OPPOSITE miter plane along the wall axis. The near-corner sliver is now a clean, positive-area, non-self-intersecting prism (outer face mitres to the corner, inner face stops at the jamb) — no spike into the void. Segments longer than the miter reach are byte-identical (clamp never triggers), so all layered/curved miter-prism walls are unaffected. The signature covers material/height/baseOffset so a legitimate material or elevation edit (join geometry unchanged) still changes it and is NEVER suppressed. The gate skips the re-armed write regardless of whether the resolver would produce a stable or an oscillating baseline (it compares the CURRENT store geometry to the last built output). Tests: `apps/editor/__tests__/wallFlushNoProgressGuard.test.ts` (2 — a wall-move-with-door converges in bounded frames, not a per-frame runaway; N no-op re-arms on unchanged geometry cause ZERO extra rebuilds while a genuine edit still rebuilds). 39 existing wall-coordinator/freeze/preserve tests + the new suite green. NOTE: furniture geometry **instancing** (the `1213 meshes → tier=performance` mesh-count explosion) is deferred — the flag-gated `§PERF-WEBGPU-FURNITURE-INSTANCING` bridge already exists in `FurnitureFragmentBuilder` and materials are already shared centrally by `MaterialService` (cache-by-colour); enabling/expanding instancing is a renderer-side change tracked with L-102. Re-routed → LOAD/PERSISTENCE agent to lazy-defer those dynamic imports off the hydrate critical path. §DIAG-EMPTY-LOAD-HANG. C05/C13.  **FIX (LOAD-PERF agent, SHA `1fc44135`):** CONFIRMED root cause = element-count-INDEPENDENT work in the setup→hydrate window (the element loops are no-ops for 0 elements). Two contributors, both removed from the empty-load critical path: **(1) Cold dynamic imports fired on EVERY load** — `@pryzm/plugin-annotations` was ALREADY statically imported (`annotationStore`, line 65), so the 3× `await import('@pryzm/plugin-annotations')` (constraintStore / annotationVisibilityStore / obcAnnotationAdapter) + the `await import('./migrations/ViewTemplateToIntentMigration')` were pointless indirection: each yields the event loop and in a prod build can trigger a lazy-chunk fetch. Converted all four to **static imports** (same module graph, already loaded → no lazy fetch, no await yield; restore logic byte-identical; DXF `@pryzm/file-format` import left dynamic + gated since it is NOT otherwise loaded and only needed when overlays exist). **(2) Frame-yielding chunked executor run for a 0-element snapshot** — chunking (C11 §6.1) exists only to spread heavy per-element geometry across frames; with no geometry the chunked driver still performs several FrameScheduler round-trips (`await yieldFrame()` per build-step), pure latency that stalls on the frame loop. New exported pure **`snapshotHasElements()`** gates the path: elementless snapshot → synchronous one-task import (no frame round-trips); any geometry → progressive chunked build unchanged. **Watchdog mislabel explained + kept honest:** `phase="setup"` came from `Object.keys(__phase_starts).pop()` naming the last STARTED boundary (setup) while the hydrate body ran; added fine-grained `§LOAD-HYDRATE-STEPS` sub-step timing so future prod loads attribute the budget precisely instead of the single coarse `hydrate` boundary. **Before/after:** empty load previously blocked ~16 s (3 watchdog cries at 7.4/10.2/15.2 s, `hydrate total=16081ms`); after, the empty path does ZERO dynamic imports and ZERO frame round-trips, so it completes well under the 7.4 s first-watchdog threshold (no lazy-chunk fetch / no frame-loop wait on the critical path). Projects WITH annotations/DXF/view-templates still restore correctly (same code, static bindings; DXF still lazy-loaded when overlays present). Test `apps/editor/__tests__/emptyLoadSkipsChunk.test.ts` (6) + `loadRedetectFreeze` (5) green; root `tsc --skipLibCheck --noEmit` exit 0. New exported fn is pure (no I/O) → no OTel span required. §FIX-EMPTY-LOAD-HANG. C05/C13. |

The 3D-bleed (elevation output appearing in the 3D scene) is the secondary correctness part. Not an empty-render bug. **RESOLVED:** adaptive NME proxy-cache cap (500 → ≥ view element set +25%, HARD_CAP 6000) so a ~1006-element elevation caches fully (no 0% thrash); re-open already instant via viewTechnicalDrawingCache. ISOLATION confirmed: elevation projection is NOT added to the 3D scene (DOCUMENTATION_LAYER / mount gate) — the 3D 'dashed bleed' is NOT the projection (likely the L-116 markers/datum lines → routed to defaultviews). Frustum/depth culling intentionally skipped (default elevations legitimately show all levels).

| L-148 | observed (founder log) | ✅ **RESIDUAL FIXED `8d2572ea`→main** (⚠ the earlier "already fixed" verdict was WRONG — the founder's live prod console still spammed the warn ~once per project on every load; orchestrator relayed the agent's claim without checking it against the log. Corrected.) **TRUE RESIDUAL, all three leads confirmed:** (a) `_setItemWithEviction` could only free `bim-project-<id>-versions` keys (`_versionKeysOldestFirst`, :297) — every other consumer was unreclaimable (`pryzm.floorPlanUnderlay.v2.*` multi-MB legacy rasters migrated to IDB only on project OPEN, `pryzm-sync-queue`, prefs), and even version blobs `warmVersionCache` had ALREADY copied to IDB were left as dead duplicates; (b) **the warms raced the sync** — `warmVersionCache()` was fire-and-forget while only `warmThumbnailCache()` was awaited, so per-project `saveProject` writes fired while the heavy legacy blobs were still resident; (c) `syncFromServer` looped `saveProject` per project, each doing a full `_serializeIndex` + `_setItemWithEviction` → a first-ever 50-project sync = **50 full-index writes and up to 50 quota warns** (exactly what the founder saw). **FIX:** new `saveProjectsBatch(upserts, deleteIds)` reads the index once, applies all upserts (routing/stripping thumbnails) + deletes, writes **exactly ONCE** (`syncFromServer` accumulates then calls it once — proven by a test counting `setItem('bim-projects-index')`); `_warmThenSync()` AWAITS both warms before the first write (purge check moved to the IDB-mirror-aware `versionRepository.getVersions(id)`, since the raw localStorage read would now miss migrated blobs and wrongly purge projects with local history); `_setItemWithEviction` gains a zero-data-loss **Tier-1 `_reclaimRedundantLegacyVersionStores()`** — drops localStorage version blobs already mirrored in IDB (authoritative copy untouched) before Tier-2 oldest-first eviction. Only this module's own keys are touched — foreign bloat (underlay rasters) is never deleted, honouring C13 + the §06 §7 single-writer rule. Genuine exhaustion now degrades with **exactly one** warn, no data loss. 34/34 persistence tests green; the single tsc error at commit time was `initTools.ts` from the concurrent furniture lane's uncommitted WIP, not this commit. **RESIDUAL (honest):** if localStorage is saturated by *live, not-yet-migrated* underlay rasters (owned by the underlay lane), the single write can still fail with one clean warn — that raster migration belongs to the underlay lane and cannot be reclaimed here without breaching single-writer. Earlier work (still valid): thumbnails → IDB (`ThumbnailCacheStore`), versions + sync queue → IDB (`VersionCacheStore`), lean metadata index stays in localStorage; migration coverage `52c12339`.   ORIGINAL prior verdict (superseded): "ALREADY FIXED (`20562684` / `36fa48ef` / `8f7ba7f7`)". ROOT: thumbnails (~5–500 KB WebP data-URLs) were stored INLINE in the single `bim-projects-index` blob (`ProjectRepository.saveProject` → `_setItemWithEviction`), so ~50 projects blew the ~5–10 MB origin cap; eviction "exhausts" because it can only delete OTHER projects' version stores, never shrink the index's own inline payload. FIX (shipped): thumbnail bytes → IndexedDB (`ThumbnailCacheStore`, sync in-memory mirror for the render path, fire-and-forget so a thumb write can't cascade into "index not saved"); version snapshots + sync queue → IndexedDB (`VersionCacheStore`, localStorage only a cold fallback); the lean metadata index stays in localStorage; quota path degrades observably (eviction-with-retry keeps the newest save + a single toast, no per-project churn); migrations MOVE legacy inline thumbs + legacy version stores into IDB then strip them, C13 isolation preserved. `52c12339` adds the 6 untested migration cases; 23/23 persistence tests green, tsc 0. RESIDUAL (cross-cutting): several suites under `apps/editor/__tests__` are not matched by the root vitest include globs, so these persistence gates don't run in CI — worth closing. ORIGINAL: **[MED — client persistence] `localStorage quota exceeded — project index not saved (eviction exhausted)` repeats on every project sync (50 projects)** — `ProjectRepository.saveProject` (main bundle) can't persist the project INDEX because localStorage is full and its eviction can't free enough; fired once per project across all 50 on load. Risk: the local project index/thumbnails don't persist → project list may not survive a reload offline, and the write churns on every sync. Thumbnails-in-localStorage is the likely bloat. Ties [[localhost-dev-unusable-test-on-prod]] persistence + L-131 P4 (IndexedDB is the primary version store). | Client persistence — ProjectRepository localStorage quota | OPEN (queued, lower priority) | route → client-persistence agent when reached: move the project index + thumbnail blobs OFF localStorage onto IndexedDB (the VersionRepository tier already used for versions), or store only lightweight index metadata in localStorage + thumbnails in IndexedDB/object cache; make eviction bounded + non-churning (don't rewrite the whole index per project). NEW PRIMARY HYPOTHESIS (agent re-engaged): **THREE.ShadowMaterial does NOT composite as transparent-shadow-only on the WebGPU/TSL renderer — it renders OPAQUE grey** (GroundShadowCatcher uses `new THREE.ShadowMaterial`; WebGPU ShadowMaterial support is known-flaky). This explains why NO shadow-map fix (setLevel/sun-refresh/autoUpdate) changed the grey — the MATERIAL is opaque on WebGPU regardless of the shadow map. FIX DIRECTION: replace the catcher material with a WebGPU/TSL-compatible shadow-catcher (TSL NodeMaterial sampling the directional shadow → alpha=shadowIntensity, transparent where lit) so the ground is invisible + only the real projected shadow shows on WebGPU, keeping the L-200 visibility gate + device-loss safety. §FIX-WEBGPU-SHADOWMATERIAL-CATCHER.   ✅ TRUE ROOT FOUND + FIXED 0489984e→main (⚠ founder live-confirm) — the ShadowMaterial hypothesis was WRONG and the founder's decisive clue disproved it: **manually swapping WebGL→WebGPU makes the grey DISAPPEAR**, so ShadowMaterial works fine on WebGPU. REAL ROOT: `RenderPipelineManager._buildPipeline()` composes the TSL graph via `createScenePass(this._scene, …)`, which runs ONCE at boot — when the scene holds NO shadow-casting geometry. Adding a caster later NEVER rebuilds the pass, so the WebGPU shadow graph never learns a caster exists → the invisible L0 catcher composites as FULLY shadowed (opaque grey plane) + no sun shadow projects. The backend swap only "fixed" it because its `recoverPipeline → activateOutlines → _buildPipeline` recreates the ScenePass against the now-populated scene. Compounding: `initScene.ts:2268` carried an explicit `NOTE: scheduleShadowRebuild() is intentionally NOT called here` — right that meshes don't own ShadowDepthTexture, but it missed that the TSL ScenePass is compiled AGAINST THE SCENE; its "Destroyed texture" fear was the `apply()` live-map realloc, already fixed in 507c12d7. FIX: initScene schedules ONE ScenePass rebuild the first time real geometry is added, LATCHED per project (reset on `pryzm-project-switch`) — meeting the old NOTE's valid "no per-event churn" concern. `scheduleShadowRebuild()` is WebGPU-gated, debounced, and coalesces in-flight rebuilds. tsc 0. §FIX-WEBGPU-SCENEPASS-FIRST-CASTER. |

The `WebGL context lost on <WebGPURendererAdapter/webgl2>` line is the **OUTGOING** webgl-fallback renderer being disposed by the swap (its context released on dispose) — a disposal artifact, NOT a live fallback. (2) **There is no autonomous swap loop** — whole-repo grep proves `swapRendererBackend`'s ONLY caller is `RendererBackendToggle._choose` on a user click. The apparent loop had two amplifiers: L-153's hub-crash made the user retry, and the device-loss safe-mode cap in `createRenderer` **silently persisted `'webgl'`**, clobbering the user's stored choice (the "forceWebGL flip-flopping"). (3) **`usedTimes` is never undefined on the pipeline** — `RenderPipeline.dispose()` → `NodeManager.delete()` reads it on internal render objects; after a swap the renderer's NodeManager belongs to a **different GPUDevice**, so `this.get(staleRenderObject)` is `undefined`. Root = disposing across a device boundary; the old `pipeline.usedTimes=0` band-aid targeted the wrong object entirely. **L-153 root:** WebGPU→WebGL builds no TSL pipeline so it can't fail; WebGL→WebGPU must re-acquire a device AND build the full TSL pipeline (`recoverPipeline`), which re-throws non-shader failures → swap returns `false` → the toggle's `_reloadInto` fired on that `false` → `location.reload()` → boots to `#/projects`. The "crash" was the reload fallback, not a dead viewport. **FIXES:** RPM records the GPUDevice each pipeline was built against and `_safeDisposeRenderPipeline` SKIPS THREE's teardown across a device boundary (resources already reclaimed) instead of walking a foreign NodeManager — band-aid removed; the toggle reloads ONLY when no live-swap entry point exists (a failed/rolled-back swap now stays on the live renderer with a non-destructive notice); the safe-mode cap no longer persists `'webgl'` (per-call `backendOverride`) so the preference can't flip-flop; initScene snapshots/restores the persisted preference on rollback. 168/168 renderer-three + 10/10 editor tests; tsc 0. ⚠ **FLAGGED TRADE-OFF (orchestrator/founder decision):** safe-mode is now session-only, so on a genuinely WebGPU-unstable machine each reload re-tries WebGPU instead of staying pinned to WebGL (favours L-203 "no silent downgrade" over L-139 persistent pinning). Revisit if heavy-scene device-loss recurs. §DIAG-FIX-WEBGPU-BACKEND-OSCILLATION + §FIX-SWAP-WEBGL-TO-WEBGPU-CRASH. |

| L-205 | founder (recurring, 6th attempt) | **[CRITICAL] Massive grey square on the first element + NO sun-projected ground shadow** — empty project renders clean; the instant the first wall is created the L0 ground catcher paints a UNIFORM GREY square over the whole 3D viewport and no shadow is projected. Founder's decisive clue: **swapping backend webgl -> webgpu makes the grey disappear.** MECHANISM (established): the catcher is a `THREE.ShadowMaterial` plane painting `opacity x (1 - shadowMask)`. Uniform grey == every fragment reads *fully shadowed* == **the key light's shadow DEPTH MAP is never rendered**. A backend swap builds a fresh `WebGPURendererAdapter` which sets `shadowMap.enabled = true` (adapter :155), which is why the swap 'fixes' it. REFUTED so far (do not re-attempt): (a) *ScenePass never rebuilt on first caster* — the path IS correct (`scheduleShadowRebuild` -> SSGI-off -> `_rebuildPipelineWithCurrentState` -> `_buildPipeline` -> `createScenePass(scene, camera)`); (b) *catcher outside the fitted shadow frustum* — three@0.183.2 `ShadowNode.js:320` does an explicit `frustumTest` and returns `float(1)` (LIT) outside; (c) *freeze latch unbalanced* — `_thawShadowAfterLoad()` runs unconditionally (initScene:2869), wall-commit freeze releases at :3190; (d) *ViewController disables shadows* — it targets the SILENCED OBC WebGL renderer by design. PRIME SUSPECT: `shadowMap.enabled` on the live WebGPU renderer is a **global GPU flag with five save/restore writers across four packages and no owner** — `BatchCoordinator:1372-1383` (stashes prior value on a **`window` global** `__pryzmBatchShadowWasEnabled` and delegates restore ACROSS PACKAGES to `CurtainWallBuilder:1389,1459` ~30s later), `initUI:1352/1396` (thumbnail capture), `initUI:2566` (user Cast-shadows toggle), `PerformanceModePanel:368`, `ShadowQualityUpgrader:184/277/375` (which is bound to the OBC WebGL renderer, so its writes never reach WebGPU). Any throw between save and restore leaks `enabled=false` **permanently**, and `RenderPipelineManager.requestShadowRefresh()` then **silently returns** (`if (!shadowMap || shadowMap.enabled === false) return;` :940) so nothing recovers it. | Rendering / shadow caster-set ownership (C04 §SHADOW; ADR-0120; ADR-0111) | **FIXED (92f437a0, live, founder-confirmed on prod).** **TRUE ROOT CAUSE (attempt 10 of 10; nine prior answers were WRONG and shipped).** `PascalSceneLighting._enableShadowsOnScene()` set `castShadow = true` on **EVERY mesh in the scene**, filtered only by whether the mesh NAME contained `edge`/`grid`/`collision`. The scene contains non-BIM meshes — notably a ground-level plane installed by the OBC `ShadowedScene`. It became a shadow caster. **A ground-level plane that casts a shadow shadows the ENTIRE L0 catcher.** Every catcher fragment inside the shadow camera then reads `shadowMask = 0` and paints `alpha = opacity` ⇒ a solid grey rectangle **bounded exactly by the shadow camera's footprint**. The catcher was kept out of the caster set ONLY by an incidental `transparent && opacity < 0.5` check — which merely `return`s, so it never CLEARED a `castShadow` an earlier pass had set. **WHY NINE ATTEMPTS FAILED — the grey rectangle is ALWAYS exactly the size of the shadow camera's ground footprint.** `±113,657 m` (runaway fitted frustum) ⇒ grey to the horizon. `±50 m` (fixed camera) ⇒ a ~100 m grey diamond. Every fix that resized the shadow camera changed the grey's SIZE and nothing else — which repeatedly LOOKED like progress. The size of the symptom was read as evidence about its cause. It never was. **THE DECISIVE EVIDENCE WAS IN EVERY LOG EVER CAPTURED**, on an EMPTY project, before any wall and before the catcher was attached: `[PBRSceneUpgrader] Applied — meshes: 2` then `[PascalSceneLighting] Shadow flags set on 1 mesh(es).` — two meshes, ZERO BIM elements, and one just became a shadow caster. **FIX (`92f437a0` §FIX-SHADOW-CASTER-DENYLIST):** explicitly **DEMOTE** (clear, never merely skip) any mesh that exists to RECEIVE a shadow (`role === 'ground-shadow-catcher'`, or any `ShadowMaterial`) and any mesh whose world bounding radius exceeds `MAX_CASTER_RADIUS_M = 500` (a 40-storey tower is ~75 m; a scene/ground plane is thousands). Receivers keep `receiveShadow`. Demotions are LOGGED by name/type/role/radius so an offending mesh names itself in prod. Gate: 5 new tests (`PascalSceneLighting.casterDenylist.test.ts`) incl. the exact bug shape — a PRE-POISONED catcher must be cleared, not skipped — and a 40-storey tower that must still cast; core-app-model 262/262; root tsc exit 0. Verified in the served bundle (`domain-engine-Dygm4jwC.js`), health 200, **founder confirmed working on prod**. **THREE REAL, ADJACENT DEFECTS were found and fixed en route — none was the grey:** (1) `d9b8f7cf` an off-frame `createScenePass()` + pipeline dispose destroying the live `ShadowDepthTexture` mid-submit (`Destroyed texture ... used in a submit` ×485, climbing) — a genuine WebGPU device-loss hazard; (2) `f4533641`'s scene-AABB frustum fit deriving an **84 km** radius from a ONE-WALL scene — genuine, and the reason the grey once reached the horizon (reverted; the offending mesh is STILL UNIDENTIFIED — latent, see C04 §SHADOW.4); (3) `f3b28961` two renderers (OBC WebGL + PRYZM WebGPU) sharing one light's `shadow.map` slot — genuine, though three's `ShadowNode.setupShadow()` always creates its own target, so it was not the grey. **Finding a real bug adjacent to a symptom is not the same as finding THE bug.** That conflation cost the week. **NINE REFUTED HYPOTHESES — never re-attempt:** ScenePass-not-rebuilt-on-first-caster; catcher-outside-the-frustum; freeze-latch-unbalanced; `ViewController` disabling the live shadow map (it writes the SILENCED OBC renderer; `world.renderer` is assigned exactly once at `BimWorld.ts`); leaked `shadowMap.enabled=false`; transition-only `autoUpdate` writer desyncing across `bind()`; the off-frame ScenePass rebuild; the exploded frustum (only the grey's SIZE); WebGPU reusing a foreign `WebGLRenderTarget` (refuted from three's source). **ALSO LEARNED (real, and it invalidated several 'fixes'):** on the WebGPU node path `renderer.shadowMap.autoUpdate` is **INERT** — `ShadowNode.updateBefore()` gates on the per-LIGHT `shadow.autoUpdate`/`needsUpdate`; `renderer.shadowMap.enabled` is read once at COMPILE time (`ShadowNode.setup`). Several L-205-era fixes adjusted a switch wired to nothing. **DOCUMENTED:** `docs/02-decisions/contracts/C04-RENDERING-AND-SCHEDULING.md` **§SHADOW** (mental model; true root cause; **11 normative rules**; a **measure-first debugging protocol** whose step 3 — `console.table` every `castShadow` mesh sorted by radius — would have ended this in minutes; the nine refuted hypotheses) and **ADR-0120** (shadow caster-set ownership: explicit, receiver-never-casts, size-bounded, self-logging). **RESIDUAL, ACCEPTED:** the ground shadow is now correct but **SOFT** (`metresPerTexel ≈ 0.195 m` at ±50 m / 512²). Sharpening is deferred to the ordered, measured path in C04 §SHADOW.4 — raise `mapSize` ONCE at allocation on the STABLE camera (512→2048 ⇒ 0.049 m/texel, 4×), device-safe + tier-gated; a clamped fit only after. **This is the exact request whose careless implementation started L-205.** |

The founder's own log confirms it live: `Phase: phase4 \| WebGPU: true \| SSGI: false \| TRAA: false`. Both backends therefore render the SAME standard PBR + the same shadow pass, so they are visually near-identical — the only WebGPU-exclusive effects (screen-space GI, temporal AA) are opt-in behind the RenderRail toggle and nobody turns them on. Consequence: the entire WebGPU investment is INVISIBLE in the default product, while still carrying the WebGPU-only risks (device-loss cascades, ShadowDepthTexture mid-submit, backend-swap fragility) that have generated L-139/L-153/L-197/L-200/L-202/L-203/L-205. | Rendering / product differentiation (C04; ADR-0087 recovery-downgrade) | OPEN (product decision, not a defect) | Needs a FOUNDER DECISION, not a code fix. Three coherent options: **(1) Make WebGPU visibly better** — enable SSGI and/or TRAA by default on capable devices behind the SceneQualityTier (`cinematic` already implies the intent; today it explicitly does NOT enable them), accepting the perf + device-loss risk and gating by tier/element-count. **(2) Keep parity and justify WebGPU on perf/compute** (instancing, compute pickers, future GPU solar), and stop treating it as a visual tier — then the correct default may be WebGL2 for stability, with WebGPU opt-in. **(3) Status quo** — WebGPU default, no visual delta, ongoing WebGPU-only risk with no user-visible payoff (hardest to defend). Recommend deciding this BEFORE more WebGPU-only bugfix spend. Maps C04 + the Presentation/Massing render-tier strategy (A.24). §DECIDE-WEBGPU-VISUAL-DIFFERENTIATION. |

Note `beginBatch` has the same shape (`BatchCoordinator.ts:1215` — "Ignoring."). **CORRECTION (2026-07-10, from the L-207 audit) — the orchestrator's stated cost was wrong.** I claimed the 59 `§FT-LIGHTING: lighting mirrored to legacy store` lines and `step=lighting stepMs=8998` were CAUSED by the dropped batch guards. They are not. That log line is emitted once per `lighting.created` by the bus->legacy-store bridge subscriber (`apps/editor/src/engine/initTools.ts:1762`), which is NOT gated by the batch or by `skipRedetectRooms` — you get 59 lines whether or not the batch nests. The ~9s is dominated by the settle drain plus 59 sequential `CreateLightingCommand` executions, not by the lost guards. **The REAL cost of the dropped guards is:** (a) **undo grouping** — the 59 fixtures become 59 transactions instead of one, so auto-lighting cannot be undone as a unit (this is the actual user-visible defect, and it stands); and (b) the discarded `skipRedetectRooms` opt lets room re-detection fire during the sweep. **SCOPE CORRECTION:** the OFFICE lane does NOT hit this guard — it never routes through `LightingLayoutExecutor` (no `lighting.layout-execute` emit anywhere under `office-building/`); its lighting is a direct `CreateLightingCommand` loop inside its own clean `runBatch` in a `deferWork(...,400)` frame (`officeFurnish.ts:158`), and its four furnish batches are staggered (200/300/400/500ms) so they never nest. The office structural lane DOES nest once (`OfficeBuildingExecutor.ts:1015` inside the structural batch at :369) but that is **data-safe and mirrors the accepted resi pattern** (`ResidentialBuildingExecutor.ts:679`): the outer async bracket is still open, so the inner `fn()`'s store events buffer and flush with the structural batch. Nothing is lost; it only warns. **FIX DECIDED (caller-side, NOT a re-entrant `runBatch`):** making `BatchCoordinator.runBatch` ref-counted would change global semantics and still would not make nested work a separate undo unit. `BatchCoordinator` already exposes the right primitive: `onNextSettle(...)` (contract at `BatchCoordinator.ts:796-804` — fires on the next microtask when idle, and after settle when batching). Guard the commit block at `LightingLayoutExecutor.ts:133-153` so that when `batchCoordinator.isBatching` is true it defers via `onNextSettle`, else runs as today. The lighting commit then always opens a CLEAN, non-nested batch => one undo unit (C17 DI-6 / PS-3: each dispatch is exactly one undo unit), and it is a behaviour-preserving no-op for the apartment single-level path. Keep the nesting case a hard error in dev thereafter, never a silent downgrade — `beginBatch` (`:1215`, "Ignoring.") has the same shape. §FIX-RUNBATCH-NESTING-DROPS-GUARDS. |

| L-217 | founder | **[HIGH] Stair creation does not work in plan view — the tool silently activates the 3D handler while the active view is an orthographic plan** — "check why the stair creation now is not working on plan view — maybe something is not wired?" **ROOT CAUSE (code-confirmed, and visible in the founder's own log).** His log shows the view is unambiguously a plan view — `dispatching "view-activated" event (mode="Top", type="orthographic")`, `view-selected (viewId="vd-sys-plan-l0")`, `[WallEdgeVisibilityService] Edge render mode set to 'plan'` — and yet the last line is `[StairPath3DToolHandler] activated in 3D (shape=L, groundY=0)`. But `isInPlanView()` (`packages/core-app-model/src/views/PlanView2DCreationMode.ts:46-49`) is a **conjunction**: `camera instanceof THREE.OrthographicCamera && activePlanDrawingRef.drawing !== null`. **Its name lies about its meaning.** Per its own header it answers *"is 2D snapping available?"* (orthographic camera AND a `TechnicalDrawing` mounted) — it does NOT answer *"is the active view a plan view?"*. `SlabTool` uses it correctly, as a snap-availability guard. `BimService` misuses it as a **view-mode discriminator**, so whenever the `TechnicalDrawing` is absent the stair tool concludes "we must be in 3D" and binds the 3D sketch handler over an orthographic plan camera — pointer rays resolve against a ground plane that the plan camera does not project onto, and nothing is ever created. **And the drawing IS absent:** `apps/editor/src/engine/views/PlanViewManager.ts:251` executes `activePlanDrawingRef.drawing = null` on deactivate — a **THIRD writer**, directly contradicting that ref's own contract header (`ActivePlanDrawingRef.ts:18`: *"Write access: ViewController._mountDrawing() and ._unmountDrawing() only"*). The founder's log shows exactly that teardown (`[SvpPlanToolOverlay] Detached`, `[SplitViewManager] Split view deactivated`) immediately before the stair activation. The accompanying `GL_INVALID_FRAMEBUFFER_OPERATION: Framebuffer is incomplete: Attachment has zero size` spam (hundreds of lines, then "too many errors") indicates the drawing/render-target also fails to re-mount at a non-zero size — likely why the ref stays null rather than being repopulated. **The authoritative view mode was available all along and was never consulted:** `ViewController.viewMode` (`ViewController.ts:587`) returns `ViewMode = '3D' \| 'Top' \| 'Ceiling' \| ...` and was `'Top'` throughout. **ARCHITECTURAL FRAMING:** tool routing must be a function of **view state**, never of whether a rendering artifact happens to be mounted. This is the same class as L-213/L-214 — one element type, divergent creation paths chosen by an unreliable predicate. | View state / element-creation routing (C11 one element ⇒ one creation path; C06 UI shell & tools; DOC-5.2/5.3; SPEC-STAIR-3D-CREATION) | **FIXED (f52f71f)** — §FIX-STAIR-PLAN-ROUTING-VIEWSTATE. Root-cause chain **confirmed end-to-end**; `BimService:293` was the **sole** misuser of the predicate (`SlabTool.ts:647` uses it correctly as a snap guard). Fix: routing now consults `ViewController.currentMode` via the typed `window.viewController` seam (P4-clean, `globals.d.ts`, the same accessor `ViewsRailPanel`/`GridsLevelsRailPanel` already use); the decision is a pure, dependency-free helper `apps/editor/src/engine/stairSketchRouting.ts` so it is testable without booting `BimService`. `isInPlanView()` renamed to `is2DSnapAvailable()`. **Modes decided:** `'3D'` → 3D sketch handler (SPEC #101); `'Top'`/`'Ceiling'`/`'ceiling-plan'` → plan handlers (horizontal authoring plane); `'Front'/'Back'/'Left'/'Right'` → NOT the 3D handler (a horizontal footprint is meaningless on a vertical view). **Critical detail the agent surfaced:** the elevation presets use a **perspective** projection (`ViewController.ts:932`), so *the camera alone cannot distinguish an elevation from the 3D view* — which is precisely why routing must read the view MODE, not the camera. **⚠ AGENT CORRECTION — the orchestrator's "third writer" framing was WRONG, and its prescribed fix would have broken Canvas2D snapping.** `activePlanDrawingRef.drawing` is written at **~11 sites** inside `PlanViewManager` (`:251, 543, 596, 616, 633, 658, 782, 844, 866, 995, 1015`) — it both **sets** the ref when a drawing becomes ready and **clears** it on teardown/level-switch/invalidation. PlanViewManager is the **designed owner** of the ref for the entire Canvas2D plan-render path. `ViewController._mountDrawing()` (`:1245-1249`) **early-returns** on the Canvas2D branch, leaving `_mountedDrawing` null, so `_unmountDrawing()`'s guard can never clear the ref there — ViewController *deliberately abdicates* ownership when Canvas2D is active. The two paths are **mutually exclusive**, so there IS a clean single-*active*-writer invariant, partitioned by render path. The header was simply stale. Resolved by **correcting the header** to document both owners + the mutual-exclusion invariant; deleting the write (as the brief proposed) would have broken Canvas2D snapping outright. **Zero-size framebuffer: investigated, and it is NOT upstream of the null ref.** `PlanViewManager._hideRendererContainer()` (`:263-269`) sets the main renderer container to `display:none` while Canvas2D plan is active, collapsing the WebGL drawing buffer to 0×0; continued render passes then log the GL error. That is a **frame-loop defect — rendering is not gated on container visibility** — living in `RenderPipelineManager`/`BimWorld`/frame-scheduler, i.e. **inside the L-205 no-touch fence**, so it was correctly left alone. Tracked separately. The routing bug is fully explained by the predicate misuse alone. Gate: 3 + 6 new tests green, root tsc exit 0, 7 files. | route -> view-state/element-creation agent. (1) **Route on view state, not on snap availability.** `BimService.activateStairPathTool` must ask the authoritative view mode (`ViewController.viewMode` / the active `ViewDefinition` type), not `isInPlanView(camera)`. (2) **Rename `isInPlanView()` to what it actually means** (e.g. `is2DSnapAvailable()`), and if a genuine plan-view predicate is wanted on that class, derive it from view state. `SlabTool`'s usage is a correct snap guard and must keep working. (3) **Restore the single-writer invariant on `activePlanDrawingRef`** — either `PlanViewManager` stops writing it and defers to `ViewController._unmountDrawing()`, or the ref's contract header is amended and the ownership genuinely moved. Three writers with a header claiming two is the latent hazard that made this reachable. (4) Investigate the zero-size framebuffer — a `TechnicalDrawing` mounted at 0×0 is a real defect, not just noise (see also the `[TechnicalDrawing] Layer "A-WALL" does not exist` gap noted under L-211). (5) Tests: with an orthographic plan camera and **no** mounted drawing, stair activation resolves to the PLAN handler, not the 3D handler; a 3D perspective view still resolves to the 3D handler. §FIX-STAIR-PLAN-ROUTING-VIEWSTATE. |

| L-219 | founder (2026-07-10; escalation of **L-203**, blocks **L-208**/G3) | **[HIGH — render backend] The WebGPU/WebGL toggle produces IDENTICAL pixels because BOTH positions resolve to the same backend — `webgl-fallback`. (4) Every "working" log captured during the L-205 grey-square investigation — including the runs the founder described as rendering *perfectly* — was likewise on `webgl-fallback`. **THE ARCHITECTURAL CONSEQUENCE:** if the `'webgpu'` preference ALSO degrades to `webgl-fallback` (adapter acquisition failing, the device-loss safe-mode cap `__pryzmDeviceLossRecoveryCap` latching, or the L-203 swap-loop oscillation `resolvedPreference=webgl (forceWebGL=true)`), then **both toggle positions land on the identical renderer + identical TSL/no-TSL pipeline, and identical pixels are the CORRECT and inevitable output.** The toggle would then be a no-op UI affordance that *claims* to switch backends — worse than absent, because it makes the product look like it has a rendering choice it does not have. **This is not a cosmetic issue: it invalidates L-208.** The founder's decision "make WebGPU visibly better" (SSGI/TRAA default-on, G3) is unobservable and untestable while the native WebGPU backend is never acquired — and `SSGI: false \| TRAA: false` in his log is exactly what §PERF-WEBGL2-NO-TSL prescribes for the WebGL2 path. **Do not build G3 on top of an unverified backend.** | Rendering backend (C04 rendering & scheduling; ADR-0007 WebGPU/WebGL2 dual mode; ADR-0076 §PERF-WEBGPU-FRAGMENT; ADR-0077 §RENDERER-LIVE-SWAP; L-203; L-208) | OPEN — **DIAGNOSTIC COMPLETE (read-only, no code changed). THREE stacked defects, any ONE of which alone produces identical pixels.** **⚠ THE ORCHESTRATOR'S FRAMING WAS INCOMPLETE AND ITS IMPLIED FIX WAS WRONG — the agent refuted it.** **(i) `'auto'` and `'webgpu'` are the IDENTICAL code path.** The sole consumer of the preference is `forceWebGL = pref === 'webgl'` (`createRenderer.ts:203`); nothing anywhere branches on `'auto'` vs `'webgpu'`. The toggle has **three buttons but only two possible code behaviours** — and on hardware without a working WebGPU adapter, exactly **one** observable outcome. **(ii) `'webgl-only'` is UNREACHABLE by preference.** It occurs only if `WebGPURenderer` construction itself *throws* (`RendererHandleFactory.ts:160-173`, `:198-224` → `createRenderer.ts:425`). On any machine that can make a WebGL2 context — i.e. essentially all of them — the plain `THREE.WebGLRenderer` path documented at `createRenderer.ts:59-61` is **dead code in production**. The type's three-way framing oversells a distinction the runtime almost never makes. **(iii) THE DECISIVE REFUTATION — acquiring native WebGPU would NOT make the two positions look different today.** Even the *successful* native-WebGPU boot reaches `phase4` with **outlines only**; SSGI and TRAA are **default-OFF**, opt-in via the RenderRail (`initScene.ts:2659-2674`, §FIX-SSGI-DEFAULT-OFF-TRAA-SELECT-FLASH, founder L-59). Outlines paint only on hover/selection; shadows are the same fixed ±50 m soft shadow on both paths. So a native-WebGPU frame and a `webgl-fallback` frame differ only by TSL ScenePass/tonemap vs OBC forward render — **invisible on a static, unselected grey model.** Therefore *"WebGPU is never engaged"* is REAL but **NOT SUFFICIENT** to explain the identical pixels: the default-off post-FX gating is an equal, independent cause. **Any fix that only chases backend acquisition will reproduce the founder's "still identical" complaint.** **(iv) L-203's fix HOLDS — no localStorage latch strands anyone.** The only persisted key is `pryzm.renderer.backend` (`createRenderer.ts:96`); the device-loss safe-mode cap is deliberately **session-only** via a per-call `backendOverride`, never persisted (`:294-303`, `:332-337`) — that IS the L-203 fix. But the unset default is `'webgl'` (`:143`), so **a fresh profile never even attempts WebGPU** until the user picks it. **(v) The contract violation is in the UI PROMISE, not the plumbing.** ADR-0076/0077 are honoured; the live-swap works (`initScene.ts:3740-3944`) and correctly gates TSL on `backend === 'webgpu'` (`:3846`). The footer honestly shows the **resolved** backend in its `· <backend>` suffix (`RendererBackendToggle.ts:118-124`), but the three **buttons** highlight the **requested/persisted** preference (`:99`, `:114-116`). So the user sees `[WebGPU]` highlighted next to `· webgl-fallback`, with no explanation that the request was silently downgraded — and the WebGPU button's advertised "full WebGPU pipeline: SSGI/TRAA/shadows" (`:115`) is **false by default even on hardware where WebGPU does engage.** That juxtaposition is the lie the founder is reacting to. **STILL UNMEASURED (needs the founder):** whether `navigator.gpu.requestAdapter()` succeeds on his machine — the decisive cell, cross-referenced with `backend.isWebGPUBackend`. **PROPOSED CONTRACT (agent's Option A, recommended, for the orchestrator to write into C04 + a new ADR):** collapse the dead `'auto'`/`'webgpu'` duplication and redefine the positions as **fidelity tiers** (Stable / Balanced / Cinematic) where the top tier **forces SSGI+TRAA on** and **hard-requires** `backend === 'webgpu'` — if the adapter cannot be acquired that position renders **disabled with a reason**, never silently degraded. | L-220 | founder ("audit on ALL elements — I moved a toilet in 3D but the 2D view did not update; the sofa moves, the toilet not") | **[HIGH — correctness, SYSTEMIC] The 3D transform-drag builds a DIFFERENT command payload per element type, by hand, against an UNTYPED bus — so several element types silently fail to commit their move.** **ROOT CAUSE, printed verbatim in the founder's log:** `[TransformDrag] plumbing.move failed: CommandBusError: plumbing.move: canExecute rejected — plumbingId must be a non-empty string`. So the gizmo moves the mesh visually, the command is rejected, the store is never written, and the plan view — which re-projects from the store — correctly shows the toilet where it always was. The sofa moves because `furniture.updateParameters` happens to use `id`. **The code even admits it was never verified:** the comment at `:241-243` reads *"NOTE: this commit only fires if the gizmo actually attaches to the fixture on selection — pending in-browser confirmation (harmless no-op if it does not)."* It was not harmless. **A SECOND, independent defect in the same log:** `[TransformDrag] floor.update failed: Error: [Immer] minified error nr: 18` thrown from `applyPatches` — the floor drag's inverse-patch payload (`:426-431`, `_prev.boundary.polygon`) is malformed, so **dragging a floor throws**. **THE SYSTEMIC ROOT — and the reason the founder is right to demand an audit of ALL elements:** the id key is ad-hoc per command (`plumbingId`, `beamId`, `floorId`, bare `id`…) and the dispatch site is **stringly-typed with an `unknown` payload**: `packages/runtime-composer/src/types.ts:3394` declares `executeCommand(type: string, payload: unknown): unknown`. A fully-typed `CommandRegistry` **already exists** (`packages/command-bus/src/commands.ts:4` — *"The typed contract for every command dispatched through `runtime.bus.executeCommand`"*) and **`runtime.bus` throws that typing away.** Every payload mismatch is therefore a *runtime* rejection instead of a *compile* error. **This is the THIRD founder-visible bug of this exact class in one day** — L-214 (wardrobe: Euler object into a scalar field), L-218 (carousel: same), now L-220 (plumbing: wrong key + wrong semantics; floor: malformed patch). They are one defect wearing three costumes. | Command bus typing / element transform (C03 §commands+state; C16 command authoring; P6 commands are the only mutation path; C11) | **FIXED (99f90411)** — §FIX-TRANSFORM-DRAG-PAYLOAD-AUDIT. **⚠ BOTH of the orchestrator's root causes were WRONG; the agent refuted them from source and fixed the real ones.** **PLUMBING:** the `{id, to}` payload was **CORRECT** for the legacy `MovePlumbingCommand` (takes `{id,to}`, translates a bath's start/end by the delta, updates the **geometry** `window.plumbingStore` the plan reads). The bug: a plugin `MovePlumbingHandler` (`{plumbingId,delta}`) was registered FIRST and **shadowed** the bridge (`initBusHandlers` skips on `registry.has`), rejecting `{id,to}` — AND that plugin handler mutates a **detached plugin DTO store** (`PluginRegistry.ts:351 new PlumbingStore()`) with **no bridge to the geometry store**, so the brief's "send `{plumbingId,delta}`" fix would have passed `canExecute` and **still left the 2D plan stale**. Fix: gave the working legacy bridge a distinct un-shadowed type `plumbing.moveFixture`; the drag dispatches `{id,to}` — the exact path furniture uses. **No handler guard weakened** (`MovePlumbing.ts` untouched). **FLOOR / COLUMN / BEAM (Immer 18):** immer 10.2.0 error 18 = *"Cannot apply patch, path doesn't resolve"* — **NOT a malformed patch.** The bridge declares `affectedStores:['floor']` (correct — ring-buffer undo needs it), but that also drives `attachStores` (`packages/stores/src/attachStores.ts:36`) to re-apply the FORWARD patch to the bus's `floor` store, which is a **detached empty `new FloorStore()`**; `Store.applyPatch` runs `applyPatches({}, [replace /floorId/boundary])` → id doesn't resolve → throw → the whole `executeCommand` rejects. Column/beam are structurally identical. **The matrix test missed it because it wired NO `attachStores`** — the exact gap. Fix: hardened base `Store.applyPatch` to skip a nested (`path.length>1`) patch whose root id is absent (a no-op), **mirroring `ElementStore.applyPatch`'s existing `current !== undefined` guard** — a genuine latent inconsistency between the two Store impls; real plugin handlers (ids guaranteed present by `canExecute`) unaffected. **P3 (close the class):** added `dispatchTyped(bus,type,payload)` to `@pryzm/command-bus` keyed to the existing `CommandRegistry` + a `TransformDragCommands` group; migrated all 11 drag sites via a `dragDispatch()` wrapper → a wrong payload for a known command id is now a **compile error** (verified: `{plumbingId,delta}` → `TS2353`). **Honest correction:** a typed *overload* on `executeCommand` CANNOT enforce this — the loose `(string,unknown)` overload always matches and swallows the error; the correct incremental seam is a typed **wrapper** (L1 `command-bus`), which is what was built; non-drag dispatches stay on the loose path (intended residue). **P4:** `dragDispatch` raises a `pryzm:toast` on rejection, never a swallowed `console.error`. **NEW DEFECTS FOUND → L-229:** slab / grid / lighting / opening have **no 3D-drag branch at all** (dragging them silently does nothing); ceiling commits but sets no `_recordUndo` (3D-drag ceiling move not ring-captured). Doors/sofas/kitchens/beds/wardrobes/trees all use it and do **not** double-draw. `PlumbingFragmentBuilder` now tags every fixture mesh `skipInPlan: true`, so the 11,706-segment HLR load **goes away at source** rather than being overdrawn. **⚠ THE ORCHESTRATOR'S FIXTURE LIST WAS WRONG.** The real union is `packages/geometry-plumbing/src/PlumbingTypes.ts:13` → `'toilet' | 'sink' | 'urinal' | 'bidet' | 'bath' | 'shower' | 'accessory'` — **seven** types. There is **no `tap/mixer` and no `cistern` fixture type**; those are sub-parts of the toilet/shower meshes. A test explicitly asserts **elevation linework ≠ plan linework** (no silent reuse — the brief's stated hazard). **MEASURED RESULT:** before — 6 meshes edge-extracted per toilet (2108/1996/992/2396/1596/3916 faces), ~55 ms, HLR 894 → 11,706 segments. | L-222 | founder ("the elevations are not sound — really good to be honest — but when the user moves the crop view the live elevation updates, but flickering a bit. Would it be possible to update the performance and make it more organic, like flowing?") | **[MEDIUM — perf + correctness] Live crop-drag re-projects the ENTIRE elevation twice per pointermove, at two different clip depths, then knowingly displays the STALE one.** The founder reads this as flicker. It is four separate defects, all named in his own log. **(1) TWO commands per pointermove.** Each tick executes `UPDATE_VIEW_DEFINITION` **and** `SET_VIEW_CROP` (`SetViewCropCommand`), and each independently invalidates `ViewTechnicalDrawingCache` and drives a full `EdgeProjectorService.project()` + `HiddenLineRemoval` pass. Double work, every frame. **(2) The two projections disagree.** The paired `project()` calls in one tick carry **different `far` values** — e.g. `far=3.669` then `far=2.557`; `far=12.135` then `far=28.895` — because `resolveClipRange()` derives elevation depth from the crop, and the first projection still sees the PREVIOUS crop's depth. So each tick renders two different drawings. **(3) The stale one sometimes wins.** `[ViewTechnicalDrawingCache] §FIX-PLAN-BLANK-STALEGEN — accepting a stale projection into an EMPTY cache to avoid a blank view: staleGen=1206 currentGen=1209` fires on **every single tick** (1206/1209 → 1212/1215 → 1218/1221 …, generation advancing by 3 per drag step). That guard exists to avoid a blank view, and it is honest about what it does — but during a drag it means the displayed drawing is periodically the OLDER of the two competing projections. **That is the flicker mechanism, printed in the log.** **(4) The edge cache is 100% useless during the drag.** `§PERF-CACHE-STATS … cacheHits=0 cacheMisses=18 hitRate=0%` on every tick, because the cache key includes the crop-derived `far`. **But moving a crop does not change any element's edge geometry** — it changes only the clip range and which segments are classified `proj` vs `beyond` (dashed). Re-extracting all 18 groups' edges per mouse-move is pure waste; the 0% hit rate is a cache-key defect, not a law of nature. **ARCHITECTURAL FRAMING.** A crop drag is ONE user gesture, not N commands. Per **P6** the *commit* is a command (one undo entry, on pointer-up); the *live preview* between pointer-down and pointer-up is view state, not a mutation, and must not round-trip the command bus + undo stack 60×/s. Per **P3** all animation/refresh coalesces through the single frame bus — at most one projection per frame, superseded ticks dropped. And a live view must **never regress to an older generation**: hold the last good drawing until the new one is ready (the flicker is a *correctness* bug about ordering, not a *speed* bug). **`FastPathProjectorService` ALREADY EXISTS** and boots (`[main] FastPathProjectorService initialized (sub-50ms interactive projection)`) — the "organic, flowing" behaviour the founder wants is progressive refinement: fast path while dragging, full-quality projection on settle. | Views / documentation projection + scheduling (C04 §scheduling, **P3** single rAF / frame bus; **P6** commands are the only mutation path; C06; DOC-1.4/1.8) | **FIXED (078fa550)** — §PERF-ELEV-CROP-DRAG-FLOW. Root cause D1/D2 confirmed: the "crop drag" is a section/elevation **scope-box depth-handle** drag; `PlanViewInteraction._applyScopeDragFromPointer` (`:1139-1157`) fired **two** legacy `commandManager.execute` commands per throttled pointermove (`view.updateDefinition` + `view.setCrop`), each dispatching `vd:view-updated` → `PlanViewManager._onViewUpdated` (`:855`) reprojected on EACH → two projections per tick; the first (from `updateDefinition`, before `setCrop` ran) saw the **stale** `far` via `resolveClipRange` (`EdgeProjectorService.ts:2856`) — **so D2 is caused by the two-command split, and P1 subsumes P3** (one atomic write ⇒ one projection ⇒ one consistent `far`). Fix: live drag now does a **direct view-state write (0 undoable commands)**, committing ONE `view.updateDefinition` on pointer-up (**undo entries per gesture: dozens → 1**); projections frame-coalesced (superseded dropped); the drawing cache holds the **warm** drawing on a generation bump (`ViewTechnicalDrawingCache.bumpGeneration`) so a superseded projection hits the reject path, never the stale-accept path — **displayed generation is now monotonic** (the flicker was non-monotonic display), and the camera no longer refits every tick. **§FIX-PLAN-BLANK-STALEGEN (L-90) PRESERVED** — the empty-cache blank-view guard is untouched and green (4/4); only the drag-time stale *display* was removed. **P6 (FastPathProjectorService) NOT routed** — it is hard-gated to `OrthographicCamera` plan previews (`:149`) and draws raw `EdgesGeometry` to an overlay; elevations render `OBC.TechnicalDrawing` into the Canvas2D pane, and its raw-edge output would change the look mid-drag (forbidden). "Flowing" delivered by the coalesce + hold-last-good instead. **⚠ ORCHESTRATOR CORRECTION — P5 (edge-cache key) was a WRONG root cause; the agent refuted it and deferred rather than regress.** `_cwProjectionCache` does NOT store crop-invariant edge geometry — it stores **post-classification drawing-space geometry** (proj-vs-`beyond` banding under the crop-derived `far`), and the `clipSignature` including `far` **IS L-202's correctness fix** (`:1198-1250`); removing `far` from the key would replay geometry classified under the OLD crop and reintroduce the L-202 defect. The sound version is a **two-level cache** (crop-invariant extraction keyed on element+version, then cheap per-crop re-classification) — a real refactor, deferred → **L-228**; the `hitRate=0%` during drag is now harmless because the warm drawing renders every frame. Gate: core-app-model views 49/49 (incl. preserved planBlankStaleGen 4/4), geometry-plumbing 16/16, root tsc exit 0, 6 files. | route -> views/projection agent. **Sequence AFTER L-221** — that agent currently owns `EdgeProjectorService`. (1) **One gesture ⇒ one command.** Collapse the `UPDATE_VIEW_DEFINITION` + `SET_VIEW_CROP` pair; commit ONE `SET_VIEW_CROP` on pointer-up (one undo entry). Live drag updates view state / the projector directly, not the command bus. (2) **Coalesce to the frame bus (P3):** at most one projection per frame; drop superseded ticks; never run two projections for one tick. (3) **Fix the clip-range ordering** so a projection can never run against the previous crop's `far`. (4) **Never display a stale generation.** Replace the `§FIX-PLAN-BLANK-STALEGEN` drag behaviour with hold-last-good-drawing (double-buffer): swap only when the new generation is ready. OBC/Pascal submit during the async rebuild? the shadow-map reallocation itself?). "External Instance reference no longer exists" is a Dawn instance teardown = the whole GPU device died. **(B)** Cesium cannot survive the device loss — after WebGPU is lost/recovered, Cesium's WebGL context is left invalid (`Compile log: null` = lost/reset GPU process), so the globe toggle hits a shader-compile failure and Cesium halts with no recovery. **NOTE:** the L-205 grey-square investigation that froze this subsystem is RESOLVED (C04 §SHADOW + ADR-0120 shipped); this is a DEVICE-LOSS crash in the ADR-0111 domain, so the freeze is **LIFTED for L-231** (fix must still honour C04 §SHADOW: never resize a live map, never dispose off-frame). | Rendering / WebGPU device loss (C04 §SHADOW; **ADR-0111**; A.24) | **FIXED (068ff74d).** **MEASURED CAUSE - two compounding defects, both named in C04 §SHADOW.** **(1) The rebuild never paused rendering (rule 7).** The pass holding the stale handle is the **TSL ScenePass shadow depth pass driven from `rp.render()`** (`RenderPipelineManager.ts:551`) - the live pipeline's own per-frame WebGPU submit; NOT OBC, NOT Pascal, NOT the recovery dispose. `scheduleShadowRebuild`'s *normal* branch (`:795`) calls `_rebuildPipeline()`, which clears `_hasPipelineError = false` **synchronously at its top (`:2031`), before awaiting the async work** - so the render gate (`:523`) stayed **OPEN for the entire ~6 s rebuild** and kept submitting frames while `_safeDisposeRenderPipeline()` (`:1721`/`:1851`) tore the pipeline down under them. The **ground hides this**: normalized by the full-day count its step is `1/N ~= 2.9%` at the 25-min cadence - below the banding threshold. The MOVE_WINDOW mechanism was exactly right: wall re-lifted (listed), room re-detect rebuilt 526 rooms + 263 labels (unlisted) -> 789 roots dropped. **Type-agnostic fix:** a `childadded`/`childremoved` listener pair on the **THREE scene root**. The agent first REJECTED the alternatives with evidence - there is no bus wildcard (`EventBus.on` is name-keyed; `EventCatalog` is a compile-time interface, not runtime-enumerable), and `pryzm-batch-ended` is **insufficient** (it only fires from batchCoordinator's bulk-generation lifecycle; a single gizmo move -> WallRebuildCoordinator flush -> room redetect does NOT go through it, so it would have missed the founder's exact scenario). The invariant used instead: **every level-tracked root is a direct child of the scene root**, and a rebuild is always `scene.remove(old)` + `scene.add(new)` (verified across WallFragmentBuilder/Slab/Column, InstancedMeshCoalescer, and critically `RoomLabelRenderer` - the 263 labels). One listener pair catches every rebuilt root of every type, **including future types, with no list**. The perf trap the old allowlist accidentally avoided (selection outlines / TransformControls helper triggering a whole-scene re-bucket on every hover) is closed with two **type-knowledge-free** gates: skip when mode is `stacked` (offsets are 0 - nothing to fix), and skip nodes with no `levelId`/`id`/`roomId` (precisely the trackability predicate `_buildLevelGroups` already keys on). **P4 (the §SELECT-GIZMO-REATTACH per-frame flood) FIXED without touching SelectionManager** - the flood's proximate cause was inside the explode controller: `_refreshSelectionAnchor` emitted `pryzm-reanchor-transform` unconditionally with a mesh the builder had just `scene.remove()`d, re-arming the very stale binding `SelectionManager.guardTransformControlsAttachment()` had just torn down (which is why the guard's warning appeared *alongside* the flood rather than preventing it). Added a detached-check + bail. `preservedBaseY` untouched, now with explicit regression cover. Gate: 25/25 across all three level-explode suites, root tsc **exit 0**, 2 files (+437/-35). | route -> view/inspect agent. **Do NOT just add the missing event types to the allowlist** — that perpetuates the smell and the next element type drops again. | L-237 | founder (the L-205 grey rectangle, with DECISIVE new evidence) | **[HIGH] The grey rectangle appears EXACTLY when the first caster is created, and a renderer swap in EITHER direction fixes it permanently.** Founder: *"when project starts the canvas is white, the 3D renders perfect.
