Then I switch back to WebGPU - also renders perfect, all walls with the shade projection on the invisible ground layer, sound. However during the first element creation and until I switch, the wrong grey rectangle is present."* **THIS IS THE MOST DIAGNOSTIC EVIDENCE THE L-205 FAMILY HAS EVER HAD**, and it closes the mechanism. **(1) THE TRIGGER IS THE CATCHER-REVEAL, NOT A ROGUE CASTER.** `RealEnvironmentService` deliberately keeps the ground catcher **attached but INVISIBLE until the first caster** (`_applyCatcher` -> `this._ground.setEnabled(this._hasCasters)`, `:184-189`; boot log: `ground=on (catcher attached; hidden until first caster)`). That visibility gate is itself a **workaround** for the grey on an empty project. The moment the first wall lands, `updateGroundCatcherVisibility()` reveals the plane - and the grey appears. **(2) THE CODE ALREADY CONFESSES THE REAL BUG.** `RealEnvironmentService.ts:141-143`, verbatim: *"(The cosmetic empty-project grey - **ShadowMaterial not fully transparent where unlit on WebGPU** - is a SEPARATE follow-up that must NOT touch this receive path.)"* So a known, never-fixed WebGPU ShadowMaterial compositing defect is on record; the caster-visibility gate merely HID it on an empty scene and it re-surfaces the instant the catcher is shown. **(3) THE MECHANISM (C04 §SHADOW.0).** The catcher paints `alpha = opacity x (1 - shadowMask)`. Lit => `shadowMask=1` => alpha 0 => invisible. If the shadow map is **not validly bound / not yet rendered** when the catcher first composites, every fragment reads `shadowMask = 0` => `alpha = opacity` => **a solid grey plane bounded by the shadow camera** - which is exactly the founder's rectangle. **(4) WHY THE SWAP CURES IT.** `pryzmSwapRendererBackend` rebuilds the whole renderer + TSL pipeline, recompiling the ShadowNode material against a freshly-bound shadow map. After that the mask resolves correctly and the grey never returns - **in either direction**, which rules out 'WebGL is better' and proves the defect is a **transient invalid-shadow-map state at first-caster**, not a backend difference. (Consistent with the founder's own note: *'I don't believe WebGL renders - I think it is always WebGPU'*.) **(5) DIRECT LINK TO L-231 (just landed, 068ff74d).** The first caster fires `scheduleShadowRebuild()`. L-231 MEASURED that the normal branch of that path cleared `_hasPipelineError = false` **synchronously before awaiting the async rebuild**, so the render gate stayed **OPEN for the entire rebuild** and frames kept submitting against a pipeline being torn down and a shadow map being reallocated - **precisely the window in which the catcher would read `shadowMask = 0`.** L-231 also found the whole shadow-freeze family was writing `renderer.shadowMap.autoUpdate`, which is **INERT on the WebGPU node path** (C04 §SHADOW rule 10), so the map was regenerating uncontrolled while nominally frozen. **L-231's fix pauses WebGPU submits for the rebuild AND writes the real per-light shadow flags - it may already fix this grey.** | Rendering / shadow compositing (C04 §SHADOW.0 + rules 7/10/11; ADR-0111; ADR-0120; L-205 lineage) | OPEN (queued, HIGH) - **RE-TEST ON THE L-231 BUILD FIRST** | route -> rendering/shadow agent. **STEP 0 (founder, 1 minute): re-test on the deploy containing L-231 (068ff74d).** If the grey is gone, this is closed by L-231 and the remaining work is only the regression test. **If it persists:** (1) The defect is the catcher compositing against an **unbound/unrendered shadow map** at the first-caster transition. The sound fix is an **ordering guarantee**: the catcher must not be made visible until the shadow depth map has actually been rendered at least once WITH the new caster - i.e. gate `setEnabled(true)` on 'shadow map valid', not merely on 'a caster exists'. (2) **Do NOT re-litigate the refuted L-205 hypotheses** (C04 §SHADOW.3 lists nine; the caster-denylist and the shadow-camera size are NOT the cause - resizing the camera only ever changed the grey's SIZE). (3) Investigate the recorded WebGPU `ShadowMaterial` transparency defect (`RealEnvironmentService.ts:141-143`) on its own terms - is `ShadowNodeMaterial`/`ShadowMaskModel` compositing correctly when the depth map is empty? Per C04 §SHADOW.11 the frustum test returns LIT outside the frustum, so an **empty/unrendered map inside the frustum** is the suspect state. (4) The caster-visibility gate is a WORKAROUND masking a real bug - once the compositing is correct, consider whether the gate is still needed. (5) Tests: creating the FIRST caster in a fresh project does not produce a fully-opaque catcher; the catcher's alpha is 0 wherever lit, on the WebGPU path, WITHOUT a renderer swap. §FIX-GREY-CATCHER-FIRST-CASTER-UNBOUND-SHADOWMAP. |

IT IS A DEAD KNOB.** The user can already pick Coarse/Medium/Fine in the UI **and it changes nothing on screen**: schema `packages/schemas/src/view/view-template.ts:224` (`z.enum(['Coarse','Medium','Fine']).default('Medium')`); view store `packages/core-app-model/src/views/ViewDefinitionTypes.ts:245` (`detailLevel?: 'coarse'|'medium'|'fine'`); command validator `packages/command-registry/src/views/SetViewOutputCommand.ts:57-58`; defaults `DefaultViewsManager.ts:279/302/329` (every default view ships `detailLevel: 'medium'`); **a LIVE dropdown** at `apps/editor/src/ui/ViewPropertiesPanelBuilders.ts:258-265`; a lockable view-template field (`ViewTemplateManagerPanel.ts:26`); a template-sync case (`SyncStateEngine.ts:315`); an intent migration mapping (`ViewTemplateToIntentMigration.ts:83`). **Every layer of the plumbing is built. NO SYMBOL BUILDER READS IT.** So the founder's feature is ~70% already in the tree - what is missing is exactly the CONSUMER. **This is the same dead-wiring class as L-224** (a listener on the wrong bus) and **L-219** (an inert renderer toggle): a control the user can operate that is not connected to anything. **FINDING 2 - THE ENUM IS FORKED (a real latent bug).** `schemas` says **`'Coarse'|'Medium'|'Fine'`** (capitalised); `core-app-model` and `SetViewOutputCommand` say **`'coarse'|'medium'|'fine'`** (lower-case). **Two competing spellings of the same domain enum across a layer boundary** - a P5/C03 violation (schemas are the single source of type truth). Any round-trip through both will mis-compare. **This must be unified BEFORE the consumer is written, or the consumer will silently never match.** **FINDING 3 - the current symbol (`packages/geometry-door/src/DoorPlanSymbolBuilder.ts`, 437 lines).** It is already doing the RIGHT structural things and already has a **lineweight hierarchy** (`:95-96`: `A-DOOR-CUT` -> leaf rectangle, HEAVY, cut by the section plane; `A-DOOR-PROJ` -> swing arc + open-position line, LIGHT), a 32-segment arc (`:37`), correct double-leaf handling (`:123-125`), and the L-127 fix that resolves frame/leaf thickness from the SELECTED DOOR TYPE so the symbol matches the placed 3D door exactly (`:232-238`). **So dimensional truth is already guaranteed and MUST BE PRESERVED.** What the founder's image 1 (current PRYZM) shows against his image 2 (target) is a DRAUGHTING gap, not a geometry gap: (a) **no wall poche** - the cut wall is a thin outline, so the drawing reads flat and the door does not read as a hole in mass; (b) **the lineweight hierarchy is too weak to see** - cut and projection look nearly identical on screen; (c) **no frame reveal / jamb rebate detail** at Fine; (d) **no door hardware (lever)** at Fine; (e) the arc + open-leaf + closed-leaf lines together read as a confusing extra chord. | Drawing / plan symbols + view detail level (**C09**; **C03/P5**; **Contract-23 §8** pen table; C06; L-127 lineage) | **FIXED (47e74e99)** - awaiting merged-HEAD tsc + push. **THE BIG FINDING IS NOT ABOUT DOORS: THE ENTIRE PEN TABLE WAS DEAD - EVERY ELEMENT TYPE, EVERY PLAN VIEW.** In `PlanViewCanvas.render()`, TWO bugs cancelled the whole Contract-23 §8 line-weight system: **(1)** `const materialLineWeight = Number(baseMaterial.linewidth)` - THREE's `LineBasicMaterial` **defaults `linewidth` to 1**, so every `LineSegments` child *looked like* it carried an authored weight of exactly 1; **(2)** `ctx.lineWidth = Math.max(hairline, vgLineWeight * hairline)` **unconditionally OVERWROTE** the pen table's `_pen.widthMm * SCREEN_PX_PER_MM` - and the VG resolver ALWAYS returns a weight (`BUILT_IN_DEFAULT.lineWeight = 1`), so it ALWAYS fired. **A 0.50 mm wall CUT and a 0.18 mm door PROJECTION both collapsed to `1 x hairline`. Identical. Every line in every plan drawing.** (It also multiplied a *unitless* VG emphasis index by a *pixel* hairline - a units error.) **Fixed:** the pen table supplies the base width in mm; the VG weight is the **relative emphasis factor** it was always meant to be (`penPx * vgWeight / VG_BASE_LINE_WEIGHT`). Door CUT (0.35 mm) now genuinely outweighs door PROJ (0.18 mm); wall CUT outweighs both. **This improves EVERY plan symbol in the product, not just doors - and it is very likely a contributing cause of the founder's other drawing-quality reports (cf. L-221 "TOOOOO MANY LINES").** **POCHE: MY BRIEF WAS REFUTED. Wall poche was ALREADY BUILT AND WIRED** (`ISO_CUT_LAYER_TO_POCHE_FILL['A-WALL']`, `_renderPocheFills`, gated on `ViewScope.poche`). Unified in `packages/schemas/src/view/detail-level.ts`; canonical = **lower-case** (what the store, the command, the defaults and the live dropdown already spoke - so **nothing changes value at runtime**); `DetailLevelSchema` is a **tolerant reader / canonical writer**, so persisted `'Medium'` still parses and upgrades in place - **no migration script**. Window / stair / plumbing / furniture adopt it with a one-line call - the precedence is not re-implementable per type (so this cannot become a fourth if-ladder). **WHAT EACH LEVEL EMITS:** **coarse (LOD 100)** = 2 jamb ticks + 2 frame face lines + a **single-line leaf**, + the arc. **medium (LOD 200)** = frame + a **4-edge leaf rectangle at the real `leafThickness`**, + arc. **fine (LOD 300)** = frame + leaf + **reveal ticks + rebate/door-stop**, + arc + **threshold** + **lever hardware**. **DELIBERATE DEVIATION FROM MY P4 - MEDIUM IS *NOT* BYTE-IDENTICAL, AND THE AGENT WAS RIGHT TO BREAK MY RULE. | L-242 | founder ("CAN YOU REVIEW THE WALL JOINTS - THEY SHOULD BE SOUND - BUT SOMETIMES THERE ARE ERRORS - SPECIALLY AROUND T AND L JOINTS - DO THIS REALLY CAREFULLY", 2 plan screenshots) | **[HIGH - correctness] L/T joint artifacts persist because the engine that FIXES L/T/X joints BY CONSTRUCTION only runs on PLAIN walls. LAYERED walls and walls WITH OPENINGS - i.e. exactly the walls in the founder's screenshots - still fall back to the LEGACY miter path. ADR-0055 says so in writing.** His screenshots: (img 1) an L-corner with a mitre diagonal plus a small notch/sliver where the two wall faces fail to meet; (img 2) a run of T-joints between LAYERED walls that CONTAIN DOORS, with a spurious diagonal at the right-hand junction. **Both images show the two wall classes that the V2 pipeline does not handle.** **ROOT CAUSE - THE FIX WAS SHIPPED FOR THE WRONG HALF OF THE WALLS (documented, not speculative).** ADR-0055 (Pascal-style wall junctions) built `JunctionResolverV2` + `WallFootprint2D` + `WallPolygonExtruder`, which produce **edge-coincident corners at L/T/X junctions BY CONSTRUCTION - no wedge, no overlap, no infill prism**. It is **DEFAULT-ON** (`WallPipelineV2.ts:44` `isWallPipelineV2Enabled()`; escape hatch `window.__pryzmWallPipelineV2 = false`). **But it is wired into ONE call site only:** `WallFragmentBuilder.createWallBodyFragment` - and ADR-0055 P3b states that site is *"(non-layered, no-openings - the simplest call site)"*. **ADR-0055 P4 - 'Extend V2 to the layered + opening call sites; delete `WallJunctionInfill*`' - is BACKLOGGED.** Verbatim, ADR-0055:118: *"The current ship state (P1+P2+P3a+P3b, default-ON) already closes the wedge for the **dominant** production case (plain partition walls)... **Layered + opening junctions retain `WallJunctionInfill` as the interim mitigation until P4a/P4b ship.**"* And the contract-sync audit repeats it: *"the layered-and-openings path still routes through the legacy `WallJunctionInfill`."* **So there are TWO join engines live in the same scene, and which one a wall gets depends on whether it has layers or a door in it.** That is the defect class, and it is why the founder's report is intermittent (*"SOMETIMES there are errors"*): a plain wall joins cleanly, the layered/doored wall beside it does not. **WHY THE LEGACY PATH SPECIFICALLY FAILS AT T AND L.** ADR-0055's own rationale for making V2 default-ON records that running every wall through the legacy `MiterPrismBuilder` *"made the 3-WALL (T/X) JOINS WORSE: legacy **over-extends at complex junctions into degenerate dark slivers**"* (founder, 2026-06-19: *"black shapes appearing in joins, often 3 wall joins, got worse"*) - `WallPipelineV2.ts:33-42`. The legacy path is a **prism-overlap + `polygonOffset` infill hack** (`WallJunctionInfill*`, `MiterPrismBuilder`), which patches junctions rather than solving them; the residual slivers/notches ARE its known failure mode. **The founder is seeing the legacy engine's documented failure mode, on the walls that never got migrated.** **FOUR JOIN SUBSYSTEMS COEXIST IN `packages/geometry-wall`:** `JunctionResolverV2` + `WallFootprint2D` + `WallPolygonExtruder` + `WallPipelineV2` (the V2 solve); `WallJoinResolver` (baseline trimming, carrying accreted patches: §MULTI-CLUSTER, §WJR-INVALID, §WJR-DIFF-THICKNESS, §PARTITION-SHELL-INNER-FACE); `WallJunctionInfill` + `WallJunctionInfillManager` + `MiterPrismBuilder` (the legacy prism/infill patch); `WallJunctionClustering`. **ADR-0055 P4c exists precisely to DELETE the third group.** **CROSS-LINKS - this is the hub of a cluster of open tickets, not an isolated bug.** **L-238** (the founder's *"corrupted triangular shape created in wall joints of 3 walls in L or T shape"* leaking into a NEW project) is almost certainly the SAME artifact, and the same `§V2-SPIKE-GUARD` / `§WJR-INVALID` degenerate-mesh family (`WallFragmentBuilder.ts:880-900`, `:2963`). **L-239** (layered walls) is the same population of walls. **L-234** (wall-move freeze) lives in `WallJoinResolver.resolveLevel`. **The known multi-cluster degenerate-wall bug** (guard skips the degenerate wall but its MESH still renders) is recorded in the plan at `V1-LAUNCH-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md:2314`. | Wall junctions (**ADR-0055** P4a/P4b/P4c - the migration this ticket demands; ADR-0055A; C11; §WJR-INVALID; §V2-SPIKE-GUARD; L-238 / L-239 / L-234 cluster) | **INVESTIGATED (read-only, numerically reproduced against the REAL resolvers). ROUTING CLAIM CONFIRMED; MECHANISM CLAIM REFUTED - AND THE TRUTH IS FAR WORSE: WE LOBOTOMISED THE LEGACY JOIN ENGINE OURSELVES, NINE DAYS AGO.** **THE HEADLINE - A REGRESSION WE SHIPPED (2026-07-02).** The legacy L/T corner fixes work **exclusively by MOVING BASELINE ENDPOINTS** (they set **no miter normal at all**: reproduced, `MN start=NULL end=NULL`). `§FIX-WALL-JOIN-BASELINE-IMMUTABLE` (**L-44/46/47, shipped 2026-07-02**) now anchors every moved, authored-valid wall back to `_sourceBaseLine` (`WallRebuildCoordinator.ts:1405-1424`, `_baselineImmutable`, **DEFAULT ON**) - **and therefore DISCARDS every one of those corrections.** The legacy join engine has been silently lobotomised. **V2 walls were immune, so nobody noticed.** **REPRODUCED, different-thickness L-corner** (A t=0.30 -> B t=0.10 at (5,0)): the resolver extends A to x=5.050 and trims B to z=0.149; **both moves are discarded**; both walls render square-capped at the authored endpoints. Result: the rectangle **x in [5.000, 5.050] x z in [-0.150, 0.000] is covered by NEITHER wall - an open 50mm x 150mm notch at the outer corner. THAT IS THE FOUNDER'S IMAGE 1.** Simultaneously B's cap is buried 150 mm inside A -> a 76.5 cm2 overlap whose cap edge draws the spurious line across A in plan. **AND THAT IS WHY IT IS *SOMETIMES*:** an **equal-thickness** L uses a **miter normal** (`MN end=set`), moves no baseline, is untouched by the anchor, and renders a **perfect shared 45deg miter**. **The defect is keyed to THICKNESS INEQUALITY** - and layered walls are exactly the walls whose thickness differs from the plain partitions beside them. The legacy resolver's own comment (`WallJoinResolver.ts:2083-2086`) names the very defect it exists to prevent: *"the convex OUTER quadrant of the corner ... is filled by NEITHER wall: an open notch at the building's outer corner."* **We disabled the fix and the notch came back.** **THE T (image 2):** all three walls at a 3-wall cluster get `MN = NULL` and **no trim** - the guest's trim onto the host face is a baseline move, **discarded** - so a thin stem starts on the bar's CENTRELINE and pokes 150 mm into it, drawing its outline across the bar's inner face. The spurious **diagonal**: opening-bearing walls render via the SEGMENTS path, and `MiterPrismBuilder` receives an MN computed against the TRIMMED baseline but applies it at the UN-TRIMMED endpoint -> the miter plane lands at the wrong station; on a short end-segment (door near the junction) that cut slices diagonally across the segment. (Same family as ADR-0055 §MITER-SEGMENT-CLAMP / L-93.) **THREE OF MY OWN ASSUMPTIONS REFUTED:** **(i)** `WallJunctionInfill` is **NOT** the producer of the L-notch - it **never fires at an L** (`WallJunctionInfill.ts:77`: `if (wallIdsInCluster.length < 3) continue;`). **(ii)** The infill is **invisible in plan view** entirely (it is added to the scene root with **no element id**, and `EdgeProjectorService` projects only meshes in the `elementRegistry`) - **both founder screenshots are plan views, so NEITHER shows an infill prism.** Retiring the infill (P4c) is a **3D cleanup**, not the fix for these screenshots. **(iii)** My proposed P4a option (b) *inset-from-envelope* is **also broken** - cross-wall edge-coincidence still requires every wall at the junction to share a layer structure; a 3-layer partition meeting a 5-layer shell has no layer correspondence to be coincident with. **ADR-0055A already analysed this and chose Algorithm C** (ONE V2 envelope prism at full thickness + per-layer `geometry.groups` material bands). It is right, and it is safe: IFC composition is stamped from `wall.layers` (not derived from the mesh), and plan layer lines come from pure math (`WallLayerPlanLines.computeWallLayerLines`), so neither output depends on per-layer solids. **BONUS BUG:** the infill is fed a **filtered population** (`WallRebuildCoordinator.ts:1732-1737` filters to `openings>0 || layers>0` BEFORE clustering), so a junction whose third wall is PLAIN drops below 3 and gets **no infill at all** - mixed junctions are left with an unplugged void. And in 3D the infill is inflated 25 mm past the wall faces (`§JUNCTION-INFLATE`) with `polygonOffset(-1,-1)`, so it **wins the depth test against the walls it protrudes through** - a self-inflicted 3D artifact. **L-238 PRODUCER NAMED:** three different junction-clustering radii coexist - **V2 = 0.20 m** (`JunctionResolverV2.ts:123`), **legacy = 0.12 m**, **infill = 0.5 m** (`WallJunctionInfill.ts:31`). A wall shorter than V2's band gets BOTH endpoints in ONE cluster -> the ring sweep hinges both ends on one pivot -> a **bow-tie, negative-area (inverted-normal) polygon -> renders BLACK from every camera** (ADR-0055:182, signed area -0.03). The legacy 0.12 m band never doubles it, so `§WJR-INVALID` never fires. **Fix the producer** (unify the three bands onto one constant; refuse a sub-band stub AT COMMIT), not the cleanup. **THE LOAD-BEARING CONCLUSION:** *the legacy engine's L and T corrections are implemented as ENDPOINT MOVES; endpoint moves are now FORBIDDEN (correctly - L-44/46/47, and the reverted §CLAMP-COSHARE-WELD which surfaced DOUBLED walls); and **V2 is the ONLY engine in the codebase that produces watertight corners WITHOUT moving an endpoint.*** **Finishing ADR-0055 is therefore not the tidy option - it is the only option that satisfies both invariants at once. P4 is promoted from an enhancement to a CORRECTNESS PREREQUISITE.** **DO NOT 'FIX' THIS BY RE-ENABLING THE BASELINE MOVES** - that is the shortest path and it walks straight back into doubled walls. (Constraint relayed to the live L-234 agent, which owns `WallRebuildCoordinator.ts`.) | Wall junctions (**ADR-0055** P4a/P4b/P4c + **ADR-0055A** Algorithm C - `status: proposed`, MUST BE ACCEPTED FIRST; §FIX-WALL-JOIN-BASELINE-IMMUTABLE L-44/46/47; §WJR-INVALID; L-238 / L-239 / L-234 cluster) | OPEN (**HIGH - now a CORRECTNESS prerequisite, not polish**) - design complete, fix not yet dispatched | route -> wall-junction agent. **(1) THE ANSWER IS ALREADY WRITTEN: SHIP ADR-0055 P4a + P4b.** Do not invent a new approach and do not add a fifth patch to the legacy infill. The architecture already decided that V2 is the correct model and that the infill must be RETIRED (P4c). This ticket is the business case for finishing that migration. **(2) P4a (layered walls, no openings)** - ADR-0055:110 already names the hard part: `WallMiter` stores absolute world-XZ corners computed against the wall's HALF-THICKNESS, so a layered wall cannot reuse one wall-level miter (each layer has a different lateral offset + thickness). ADR offers **(a)** run `resolveJunctions` once per layer offset, or **(b)** derive per-layer corners by INSET from a single wall-level envelope. **(b) is the architecturally sound one** - one junction solve remains the single source of truth and layers are derived from it; (a) creates N independent solves that can disagree at a corner. **This requires an ADR-0055 ADDENDUM - write it, do not decide it in code.** **(3) P4b (walls with openings)** - ADR-0055:111: the END segments abutting a junction need a polygon footprint that also respects the opening's left/right edges (a polygon-vs-rectangle carve). **Note this interacts with the L-234 fix** (which is about how OFTEN we rebuild these bodies) - coordinate, do not collide. **(4) P4c** - delete `WallJunctionInfill*` + the `polygonOffset` hack, gated on P4a+P4b verification. **The `polygonOffset` patch is a rendering band-aid over a geometry defect and must not survive V1.** **(5) DO NOT TOUCH `§CLAMP-COSHARE-WELD`** - that corner fix was REVERTED because moving shared baseline endpoints surfaced DOUBLED walls; any new corner work must not move endpoints. **(6) Tests: a golden-plan junction matrix** - {L, T, X} x {plain, layered, opening-bearing, layered+opening} x {same thickness, different thickness} - asserting NO sliver, NO notch, NO negative-area polygon, and edge-coincident faces. 'I'; const requestedWidth = config?.width ?? …; const typeId = config?.typeId;` - and the comment says it plainly: *"read the architect's chosen shape from the global **stamped by BimService.createStair's onConfirm**. That is a sound INVARIANT, but it makes the upper level a **hard input**. THIRD REFUTATION TODAY.** The agent drove the real tool end-to-end in a fresh single-level project and probed it: `PROBE ONE-LEVEL executeCalls=0 warns=["[StairPathToolController] Cannot finish: invalid - Riser too small (0 mm - min 100 mm)"]`. **The stair does NOT die at the `topLevelId` abort I cited.** **THE HANDLER I WROTE THE ENTIRE BRIEF ABOUT IS NOT THE ONE THE FOUNDER HITS.** The plan-view stair the ribbon actually reaches is **`StairPathPlanToolHandler`** (tool key **`stair-path`**), not `StairPlanToolHandler` (key `stair`). []`). The clip was never the broken thing.** Something UPSTREAM of it never existed at all. **THE REAL ROOT CAUSE - `A-WALL:cut` WAS *ALWAYS* EMPTY, ON EVERY WALL, IN EVERY PLAN VIEW.** The plan `:cut` layer was populated ONLY by `classifyByVertexY()`, which tags an EDGE as cut when an endpoint lies within `CUT_LINE_EPSILON` (**15 cm**) of the cut plane. **A wall solid has NO edge anywhere near a 1.2 m cut plane** - its horizontal edges sit at the base and the head, its vertical edges have endpoints only at those two elevations. **So no wall ever contributed a single vertex to `:cut`.** This is no longer a deduction, it is an assertion in the suite: `planCutSection.doorVoid.test.ts` locks `classifyByVertexY(...).cutGeo === null` for a plain wall box, so the regression cannot creep back silently. **THIS ONE FACT EXPLAINS TWO SEPARATE FOUNDER REPORTS:** (i) **no wall poche** - `_renderPocheFills` scans `:cut` for a fill to stitch and there was never anything there (*"the drawing reads flat"*, **L-241**); (ii) **no CUT lineweight hierarchy for walls** - every wall line was `:proj`. **THE FIX - CUT THE SOLID, DON'T CLIP THE LINES.** `buildPlanCutSectionGeometry()` intersects the mesh's triangles with the plane `y = cutPlaneY` and returns the TRUE section. For a wall carrying a door that is the outline of the solid **that actually exists at the cut height**: the pier left of the jamb and the pier right of the jamb, each closed on itself at the void edge. **The opening is voided BY CONSTRUCTION** - no line and no fill can cross it on ANY wall render path (plain, layered, opening-bearing, curved, CSG single-volume or V2), because at that height **the solid simply is not there**. The invariant §FIX-PLAN-DOOR-JAMB-SEAM *asserted about* the geometry (`wall face line terminus === opening void edge === frame jamb tick`) is now **OBTAINED FROM** the geometry - it cannot be violated by a render path that forgets to call the clip, **which is precisely the class of bug L-242 proved we have.** **This is also why H2/H3 no longer matter:** the new path never reads `userData.openings`, so an instanced wall, a V2 wall, or a wall whose userData was never re-stamped is cut correctly regardless. Reuses the existing `perElemLayerCutGeos` -> `:cut` -> poche plumbing already proven by section views; plan and section are mutually exclusive branches, so no wall is cut twice. Scope-guarded to A-WALL in plan (an AABB straddle-reject keeps slabs out, or the whole floor plate would paint as poche - tested). **WHAT IS PROVEN, AND WHAT IS NOT - THE L-243 LESSON APPLIED TO MY OWN WORK.** **PROVEN:** the `:cut` layer exists for walls for the first time; the door void is empty in it; the piers terminate exactly on the jambs; the lintel above the head does not re-fill the void. **NOT PROVEN:** that the **PROJECTION** lines are clipped on the founder's actual wall - that is still `_suppressPlanViewOpeningLines`, and **H2/H3 remain untested**. If the wall still reads uncut on screen after this push, **THAT is the surviving bug, and it is now isolated to one function instead of the whole pipeline.** **Gate:** 6 new plan-cut specs + 3 sibling classifier specs 9/9; geometry-kernel poche 18/18 + edge-projection 34/34 with **snapshots UNCHANGED**; **full editor suite shows NO regression** - 34 failures / 13 files, identical to a clean-HEAD baseline run I took specifically to check (pre-existing debt, all unrelated to edge projection); **root tsc exit 0**. **FOUNDER: this one needs your eyes** - the poche/cut half is proven in tests, the projection-line half can only be confirmed on screen. | route -> drawing/plan-symbol agent (the L-241 agent - it owns this code and just fixed the pen table). **(1) REPRODUCE AND INSTRUMENT FIRST. Do not trust my hypotheses.** Build the founder's exact case (a door in a wall, plan view) and log whether `_suppressPlanViewOpeningLines` runs, what `group.userData.openings` contains, and whether the cut-plane test passes. **Name the real reason before changing a line.** **(2) The invariant is already written down** (`EdgeProjectorService.ts:352`) - *wall face line terminus === opening void edge === frame jamb tick*. **Make it TRUE, and make it true for LAYERED and OPENING-BEARING walls too**, which is where L-242 proves the pipeline forks. **(3) Unify, do not add a third clip.** There are already two implementations (`_suppressPlanViewOpeningLines` + `WallLayerPlanLines`). **A third would be the fourth if-ladder.** One opening-clip rule, applied to every wall render path. **(4) The poche must be clipped too** (L-241 proved poche is built and was merely invisible; now that it is VISIBLE, an unclipped fill will read as a solid wall across the door - check this explicitly). **(5) Coordinate with L-242** - if the fix belongs in ADR-0055 P4b (the opening-bearing junction/segment carve), SAY SO and fold it in rather than patching the legacy projector. **(6) Tests: a door in a plain wall, a door in a LAYERED wall, and a door in a DIFFERENT-THICKNESS wall all produce a plan in which the wall face lines TERMINATE on the void edges, the jambs close the void, and no line or fill crosses the opening.** §FIX-PLAN-DOOR-CUTS-WALL. |

## §3 Functional-area conformance

### 3.1 Camera & navigation — **NEEDS HARDENING**
- Surface: `packages/renderer/CameraController`, `packages/view-state/ViewController`,
  `packages/stores/CameraPositionService`, `packages/renderer-three/LTPENUCameraService`,
  `runtime-composer/buildCameraControllerSlot`. Constraints armed (`minDist=0.2, maxDist=10000`, polar clamp).
- Issues: **L-02** (heavy-scene orbit slow — shadow-ceiling bypass + instancing + no nav-LOD), **L-05** (WebGL2
  ghost-on-rotate). **L-25** (WebGPU black 3D on rapid wall-create + mouse motion — `ShadowDepthTexture` destroyed
  mid-submit by the `§PERF-NAV-LOD` `castShadow` toggle) **FIXED** via `§FIX-SHADOW-MIDSUBMIT-DESTROY`: the nav lever
  now FREEZES the shadow map (`shadowMap.autoUpdate=false`) instead of clearing `castShadow`, so THREE never destroys
  the texture (no mid-submit destroy); restore is debounced; the heavy-tier drop-the-pass gate is decoupled from the
  transient nav gate. Zoom-fit/zoom-selected handlers exist (`§C-B1`).
- **N/V**: pan/zoom/orbit smoothness on mid projects; camera state persistence per view; frame-on-view-switch.
- Contract: C04 (rendering/scheduling).

### 3.2 Element creation — **MOSTLY OK, one BROKEN**
- Dual-write/bus-bridge paths verified for walls/curtain-walls/doors/windows/slabs/floors/roofs/stairs/columns/
  beams/plumbing/lighting (ADR-0098 §4, prior audit §3). Hardened door/window creation fallback (§DPT/§WPT).
- Issue: **L-09** wall-draw preview stutter (creation *interaction*, not the command).
- Contract: C11, C15.

### 3.3 Element editing — move / rotate / dimensions / materials / hosting — **CORE GAPS**
- **Movement**: wall=`UpdateWallBaselineCommand`, door/window=offset, furniture=params — non-uniform (F8).
  **L-01** host-wall move freeze FIXED (ADR-0099); **L-06** per-move storm amplifier in flight (Q6).
- **Rotation**: **BROKEN/absent** — no `Rotate*Command` (L-07/F5).
  Partial: **placement-time** rotation shipped — Spacebar rotates the live placement preview
  +90° CW (`§FEAT-PLACEMENT-SPACEBAR-ROTATE`, ADR-0107) for one-click placeables (furniture 3D +
  plan, carousel GLB), with the chosen yaw carried through to the committed element. The general
  post-placement first-class Rotate command/gizmo (F5/Q7) remains OPEN.
- **Dimensions**: OK coverage (`UpdateWall/Slab/Door/Window Dimensions/Height/Width/SillHeight`).
- **Materials**: uneven (L-08/F6) — structural/MEP/furnishings have no material command.
- **Hosting**: correct model (C15) — offset + dual-store; the perf failure (L-01) is fixed.
- Contract: C03, C11, C15, C16.

### 3.4 Selection — **BROKEN**
- `findSelectableRoot` walk-up correct; **L-04** gpu-pick chooses wrong candidate under density (Q4 agent).
- `§SELECT-STUCK-STATE-SELFHEAL` exists (escape un-wedges) — a symptom of the edit-storm (L-06), not a fix.
- Contract: C15 §11/§12, C06.

### 3.5 Views — plan / 3D / elevation / section / sheets / **view creation** — **VERIFIED (2 fixes shipped)**
- Commands present: `CreateViewDefinitionCommand`, `UpdateViewDefinitionCommand`, `DeleteViewDefinitionCommand`,
  `CreateViewTemplateCommand`/`Update`/`Delete`, `UpdateViewportScaleCommand`; `DefaultViewsManager` guarantees a
  3D + Ground-Floor plan on every project; split-view plan (Canvas2D) auto-opens; `EdgeProjectorService` +
  `HiddenLineRemoval` + `NativeElementMeshExporter` drive plan projection.
- **Traced end-to-end from the UI (§3.5 verdicts — replaces the N/V cells):**

  | Flow | Verdict | Evidence / fix |
  |---|---|---|
  | **View list / browser** (all types listed, grouped, badges, active pill) | **OK** | `ViewsRailPanel.ts` — `getByType()` per group; `vd:view-*` refresh subscriptions. |
  | **Create new plan / structural-plan view** (form + level picker + validation) | **OK** | `ViewsRailPanel._executeCreateView` → `view.createDefinition` → `CreateViewDefinitionCommand`. Level required + validated. |
  | **Create new 3D / section / elevation / detail / … view** (name form) | **OK** | Same path; `CreateViewDefinitionCommand.canExecute` whitelists all 12 `viewType`s. |
  | **Switch / activate view** (single-click; re-entry guard) | **OK** | `ViewsRailPanel._onActivateView` → `viewController.setActiveViewDefinitionId` + OBC-mode activate. Section/elevation route to Canvas2D `PlanViewManager`. |
  | **Duplicate / delete view** | **OK** | `view.createDefinition` (clone spatial) / `view.deleteDefinition`. |
  | **View range editing** | **OK** | `ViewPropertiesPanel._fireSetViewRange` → `view.setRange` (plugin-view `SetViewRangeHandler`, store-backed, undoable). Read by `EdgeProjectorService.resolveClipRange` (`spatial.viewRange.near/farOffset`). |
  | **View crop editing** | **OK** | `ViewPropertiesPanel._fireSetViewCrop` → `view.setCrop`. Read by `EdgeProjectorService` (`crop.region` / `crop.farClip`). |
  | **Elevation MARK placement** (4-dir click) | **OK** | `ElevationPlanToolHandler` → `elevation.create` → `CreateElevationMarkCommand` (creates elevation ViewDefinition + `elevation-mark` annotation with `linkedViewId`). |
  | **Section MARK placement** (2-click cut line) | **BROKEN → FIXED** | `SectionPlanToolHandler` fired `section.create`, whose bus key is owned by plugin-section-view's **geometry** handler (payload `{ line:{a,b,lookDepth} }`) — the tool's `{ sectionViewId, cutPointA, … }` was rejected at `canExecute` and swallowed by the caller `.catch()`, so **nothing was created**. Fixed **§FIX-SECTION-MARK-CREATE**: new distinct bus key `section.mark.create` (bridge in `initBusHandlers.ts`) → `CreateSectionMarkCommand`; tool repointed. |
  | **Click a placed section / elevation mark → navigate to its view** | **GAP → FIXED** | `PlanViewInteraction._onClick` only *selected* the mark; it never read `parameters.linkedViewId`. Fixed **§FIX-MARK-NAVIGATE**: on hit of a `section-mark`/`elevation-mark`, `_navigateToLinkedView()` activates the linked view (Revit click-to-navigate). |
  | **Viewport scale on a sheet** | **OK (unchanged)** | `UpdateViewportScaleCommand` + `sheet.addViewport` via `ViewsRailPanel` context-menu. |

- **Note (out of G8 lane, flag only):** the *3D* `SectionMarkTool` (plugins/annotations) additionally fires a generic
  `annotation.create` alongside `CreateSectionMarkCommand`, which can leave a redundant generic annotation. Belongs to
  the annotations subsystem (G9) — not touched here.
- The projection cost is the L-06 storm's biggest consumer.
- Contract: C04, C06, C09. Tests: `plugins/annotations/__tests__/section-elevation-mark.test.ts` (6, pins the
  mark-create + `linkedViewId` contract that both fixes depend on).

### 3.6 Annotations & dimensions — **OK (was GAP; fixed §G9-PERSIST 2026-07-02)**
- Commands: `CreateAnnotationCommand`/`UpdateAnnotation`/`DeleteAnnotation` (seen live), `UpdateElementMarkCommand`;
  `AnnotationManager` + `OBCAnnotationAdapter`.
- **OBC verdict — resolved, not a blocker.** The "linear/angle/slope annotations not present in this OBC build —
  skipping" logs are BENIGN: `OBCAnnotationAdapter` is a *secondary* front-end that gracefully no-ops when the OBC
  classes are absent. Every dimension/annotation type has a PRYZM-native tool
  (`plugins/annotations/src/tools/*`) that does NOT depend on OBC. The absent-OBC path was never the primary path.
- **Placing a linear dimension — OK.** UI button (`AnnotationRailPanel` → `toolManager.activateLinearDimAnnotation`)
  and the `D`+`I` shortcut both arm `LinearDimensionAnnotationTool`, whose commit dispatches
  `CreateAnnotationCommand` via `commandManager` into the subsystem `annotationStore` that the render layer reads and
  `ProjectSerializer` persists. Verified end-to-end. String/chained dims OK.
- **Placing a text annotation / tag / other dimension types — was BROKEN, now OK.** ROOT CAUSE found: 13 of 14
  native tools (`TextNote`, `ElementTag`, `Door/Window/Level` tags, `GridBubble`, `Angular/Slope/Radius/Diameter`
  dims, `SpotElevation`, `Keynote`, `RevisionCloud`) built the full `AnnotationElement` but only fired the bus
  telemetry `annotation.create` `{id,viewId,kind}` — which writes a DIFFERENT anchor-keyed Zustand store (per
  `initBusHandlers.ts` §P3.5-AN) and carries no geometry. They never called `commandManager.execute` /
  `annotationStore.add`, so the annotation neither rendered nor persisted. Only `LinearDimensionAnnotationTool`
  (and the mark/scale/north/matchline/datum tools) were wired correctly. **Fix (§G9-PERSIST):** new shared helper
  `plugins/annotations/src/tools/persistAnnotation.ts` dispatches `CreateAnnotationCommand` through
  `window.commandManager` (the same authoritative path LinearDim uses → store write + undo/redo); each of the 13
  tools now calls it before the bus telemetry.
- **Editing / deleting — OK.** `UpdateAnnotationCommand` / `DeleteAnnotationCommand` mutate the subsystem store with
  full undo snapshots; dimension edits route through the shared property panel (`AnnotationManager` §ANN-SEL);
  room-tag drag routes the legacy `UPDATE_ANNOTATION` through `commandManager` (initBusHandlers BUG-ANNO-DRAG fix).
- **Persistence + plan re-projection — OK.** `annotationStore.serialize()`/`deserialize()` are wired both sides of
  `ProjectSerializer`/`ProjectLoader` (`annotations` snapshot slice). Plan re-projection is reactive:
  `AnnotationRenderLayer` subscribes to `store.onChange` and re-reads `getByView(activeViewId)` through the live
  camera matrix each frame.
- **Room-tag auto-populate (`RoomTagAutoPopulator`) — OK.** Idempotent create/refresh/delete of `room-tag`
  annotations via `Create/Update/DeleteAnnotationCommand`; no-op when tags already match (verified live in house/apt
  gen).
- **Tests:** `plugins/annotations/__tests__/persist-annotation.test.ts` (9 cases) covers the helper, the
  create/update/delete + undo lifecycle, view-scoped read (render source), and serialize→deserialize round-trip.
- Contract: C06, C03.

### 3.7 Undo / redo — **OK (verify at scale)**
- History/redo stacks, per-command snapshot scope, remote/PROJECT_LOAD exclusions verified (prior audit §6).
- **N/V**: undo of a wall-move-with-openings after the ADR-0099 changes; undo during the L-06 storm.

### 3.8 Save / load & lifecycle — **BROKEN at scale**
- **L-03**: 30s false-timeout, autosave-during-load serialize (22.7MB), O(N) clear-on-switch, 940 degenerate RBLs
  (Q3 agent, C13/C05). Small projects load fine.
- Contract: C05, C13.

### 3.9 Performance & responsiveness (cross-cutting) — **BROKEN**
- **L-02** heavy nav, **L-06** edit storm, **L-09** preview stutter, **L-03** load. The shadow-ceiling bypass
  (12,737 casters at `shadows=standard`) and instancing gaps are the render-side; the redetect/plan storm is the
  interaction-side. Contract: C04, C10.
- **L-25 FIXED** (`§FIX-SHADOW-MIDSUBMIT-DESTROY`) — the `§PERF-NAV-LOD` shadow lever no longer destroys the
  `ShadowDepthTexture` mid-submit during rapid mouse motion (WebGPU device-loss → black 3D). Nav-LOD now freezes the
  shadow map (`shadowMap.autoUpdate=false`, texture kept alive) via `RenderPipelineManager.setShadowPassSuppressed()`
  with a debounced restore; prevention, not just recovery (ViewportCrashGuard remains the fallback). Honours
  `§SHADOW-DEVICE-LOSS-FIX` (no GPU texture is ever `.destroy()`-ed within the frame it is submitted).

## §4 Launch-gate summary (what MUST be green for v1)

| Gate | Requirement | State |
|---|---|---|
| G1 | No freeze on host-wall move (any element count) | F1 shipped; Q6 amplifier in flight |
| G2 | No freeze/fail opening a heavy saved project | Q3 in flight |
| G3 | Smooth camera navigation on a full building | Q2 in flight |
| G4 | Correct click-selection at any density | Q4 in flight |
| G5 | Smooth wall/element draw preview | Q8 in flight |
| G6 | No ghost/trailing on rotate (both backends) | Q5 queued |
| G7 | Move + rotate + material editable for every visible element | Q7 queued (F5/F6) |
| G8 | Create/manage plan+3D+section+elevation views from UI | GREEN — §3.5 verified end-to-end; §FIX-SECTION-MARK-CREATE (section-mark tool no longer no-ops) + §FIX-MARK-NAVIGATE (mark click-to-navigate) shipped |
| G9 | Place/edit annotations + dimensions | GREEN — native path verified; §G9-PERSIST fixed 13 bus-only tools (§3.6) |
| G10 | Undo/redo sound across all of the above | §3.7 verify at scale |

See the companion plan for the phased path to green.
